      bit-identical gate possible. It becomes necessary in M4.1b, when
      `average` turns into a coalesce that writes only the interface layer
      and nothing writes the deep interior; it has moved there.

- [x] **M4.1b — Explode/coalesce with UNIFORM distribution, DC rescale
      removed. DONE 2026-09-09. Exactly conservative in mass AND momentum on
      every geometry in the ladder.** Opt-in (`?interface=explode`); `interp`
      stays the default until 4.1c/4.1d.

      Shipped: `common_d3_amr_explode.wgsl` and
      `common_d3_amr_coalesce.wgsl` (+ q19/q27 entries), `COLLIDE_RING = 0`
      in `common_d3_amr_step1.wgsl` so interface cells advect without
      colliding, the `mac`-from-pool projection folded into coalesce, and
      `?refine=bar` as the middle rung of the geometry ladder. No
      Dupuis-Chopard rescale anywhere on this path.

      THE PASS ORDER INVERTS on this path, and that is the load-bearing
      trick: coalesce writes the fine outflux into the covered cells' slots
      of the TIME-t coarse buffer, and the coarse step then runs LAST and
      picks it up by pulling from those cells exactly as it pulls from
      anything else. No per-direction test in the hot kernel, no extra
      binding, no unfusing of stream+collide.

      **THE CONVEX-EDGE LEAK, AND THE FIX.** As first built this lost mass
      at a constant rate along every convex EDGE of the refined region --
      `box` -2.69e+1 and `bar` -1.80e+1 at t = 8, ratio 1.49 against those
      geometries' convex-edge-length ratio of 1.50, identical at RB=4 and
      RB=8, and momentum drifted with it. The cause, and it is worth
      keeping because the same shape will recur in 4.1c:

      A ring cell `p` holds, after substep B, either its interior source
      `q`'s outflux at `t + dt/2` (when `q = p - e_i` is interior) or at `t`
      (when `p - e_i` is itself a ring cell and `q = p - 2 e_i` is). The
      coalesce delivers what now sits in `coarse(p)` by writing it into the
      slot of `coarse(p) - e_i`, so the coarse pull carries it to
      `coarse(p)`. For the `t` outflux that cell is always covered --
      `coarse(p - 2 e_i) == coarse(p) - e_i` exactly. For the `t + dt/2`
      outflux it need not be: that value has moved one FINE cell, so
      `coarse(p)` may have advanced on only SOME of `e_i`'s axes, and
      subtracting `e_i` then steps back out of the refined region on the
      others. On a flat face it lands on another covered cell and the
      accounting is only smeared laterally -- which is exactly why `slab`
      measured exactly conservative and `bar`/`box` did not. At a convex
      edge it lands on the DIAGONALLY OUTSIDE cell, which is unrefined, so
      no coalesce thread claimed `p` and the value was dropped when explode
      refreshed the ring. Predicted size f/2 ~ (1/36)/2 = 1.4e-2 per edge
      coarse cell per macro-step (two grazing directions at an xy edge,
      (1,-1,0) and (-1,1,0), x two fine cells along the edge x one dropped
      population each, over the eight fine cells of a coarse cell) against
      the -1.2e-2 measured. That agreement is what identified it; the
      hand-tracing that preceded it had stopped paying.

      The fix is the ORPHAN PASS in `common_d3_amr_coalesce.wgsl`: the same
      kernel, on the cells it used to return from. Ring cell `p` in
      direction `i` is claimed by `coarse(p) - e_i`, and that cell is either
      covered (the coalesce proper) or not (the orphan pass) -- one test
      with opposite sign, so no double counting by construction. An orphan
      is ADDED to the unrefined cell's own slot: `s` is a real fluid cell
      whose `f_i` is already pulled by `s + e_i`, and the orphan is mass
      that transited `s`'s volume on its way out of the refined region, so
      it goes to the right cell in the right direction and momentum comes
      out right too.

      **REJECTED, and recorded so it is not retried: gathering by ORIGIN**
      -- summing, for each of a covered cell's own eight subcells, the two
      cells its direction-i outflux now occupies. That is also exactly
      conservative, is a smaller change, and was measured **4.6x WORSE in
      the field** (`box` whole-domain 4.58e-2 against 9.95e-3 at t = 16).
      Reason: at a convex corner six of eight subcells' diagonal outflux
      leaves the region against the coarse cell's one link, so it hands
      `c + e_i` mass that physically sits in a different neighbour.
      Conservation is necessary and not sufficient; the destination is what
      has to be right.

      GREEN, measured 2026-09-09 (`analyze-d3-interface --extra=interface=explode`):
      - `?refine=all` BIT-IDENTICAL (2.293e-3, rate -1.222e-4), which
        isolates the fine solver, the no-collide-ring change and the mac
        projection from the interface itself.
      - `?refine=slab` BIT-IDENTICAL to before the orphan pass, which is the
        evidence it fires nowhere on a flat seam: mass at the f32 readback
        floor, momentum FROZEN after the startup transient.
      - `?refine=bar` and `?refine=box`, previously the red cells: mass
        drift -2.69e+1 -> -5.85e-3 (box) and -1.80e+1 -> +3.00e-3 (bar) at
        t = 8, i.e. onto the same floor as `slab`, and MOMENTUM now frozen
        (box 6.94e-1 flat across t = 1..16, against 7.54e-1 -> 1.61e+0
        before). Mass and momentum are both exactly conservative on every
        rung of the ladder, which is what the reflux pass never managed.

      WHERE THE ERROR WENT, and why the whole-domain number rose. `box`
      whole-domain L2rel at t = 16 is 1.25e-2 against 9.95e-3 before. That
      is not a regression: the seam bucket rose (2.38e-2 -> 4.22e-2) and
      every bucket away from it FELL (+2: 1.36e-2 -> 1.07e-2, +3: 1.17e-2 ->
      6.59e-3, +4: 1.02e-2 -> 5.14e-3), so the error is now a LOCAL seam
      artifact instead of a defect radiating into the domain, and the d = 0
      bucket alone accounts for essentially all of the whole-domain figure.
      The leak was flattering the far field by removing the injected error
      along with the mass. Distributing that seam value correctly is 4.1c's
      job, and it is now the only thing left at the interface.

- [x] **M4.1c — DONE 2026-09-10. TWO changes, and the order between them is
      the finding.** The interface is now conservative AND its field error
      has fallen to the no-interface control's level.

      **M4.1c-i — the IN-ORPHAN, the mirror of 4.1b's OUT-orphan. This is
      the one that mattered.** 4.1b left the interface exactly conservative
      and NOT CONSISTENT: it did not preserve a uniform flow. Those are
      separate requirements and the geometry ladder cannot tell them apart,
      which is why the conservation gate went green over a defect worth 8x
      the field error.

      The asymmetry: at a convex edge a coarse cell's child can cross the
      seam in ONE fine step while the coarse cell itself does not, and it
      cuts both ways.

        OUT (4.1b): a half-step EXIT that no coarse bucket can absorb.
            Dropped -> a mass leak. Added to the receiving cell's slot ->
            conservative, but that cell now gets its coarse neighbour's
            f_i PLUS the orphan, i.e. more than a uniform state can hold.
        IN (this): a half-step ENTRY that no coarse cell explodes, because
            v + e_i is unrefined. Left at zero -> the fine region gathers 0
            where it should gather v's population. NOT a leak, because v
            keeps that population and streams it coarsely -- which is
            exactly the surplus the OUT-orphan then delivers.

      So the two are one defect seen from both sides, and the fix is one
      subtraction: inject v's population into those children
      (`common_d3_amr_explode.wgsl`) and take the same amount off
      `f_coarse[i][v]`. It lands on the SAME (cell, direction) slot the
      OUT-orphan adds to, and in a uniform flow the two counts are equal, so
      the net write is exactly zero -- consistency restored with
      conservation untouched. The injection is deliberately UNIFORM even
      under EXPLODE_LINEAR: only a subset of the eight children fire, so a
      linear term would not sum out and the charge would no longer equal the
      injection.

      **M4.1c-ii — the linear explosion**, as originally specified:
      `Ndot_i^f = Ndot_i^c + (r_f - r_c).F_i`, central difference on the
      axes with two REAL coarse neighbours (which excludes the interface
      normal automatically and degrades to uniform at a concave corner --
      no geometry is classified), projected orthogonal to `c_i`. That
      projection is not cosmetic: the scheme's REOCCURRENCE property is the
      statement that the exploded field is invariant under translation by
      -c_i, and a linear field is invariant under it iff `F_i . c_i = 0`.
      With a component along `c_i` the two substeps inject different values
      from one coarse state -- a spurious time variation at the seam.
      `?explin=0` restores uniform.

      **THE ORDER IS THE FINDING.** Measured on its own, against 4.1b's
      interface, the linear explosion was a NO-OP -- box whole-domain
      2.19e-2 vs 2.23e-2, well inside run-to-run noise. It was completely
      masked: the edge inconsistency is O(1) and swamped a second-order
      term. With the IN-orphan in, the SAME change is worth a factor of two
      on every geometry. A stage that measures as a no-op is not necessarily
      wrong; it can be downstream of something bigger.

      MEASURED 2026-09-10, RTX 4080, Beltrami box, whole-domain L2rel at
      0.5 t_d, N=48 (`analyze-d3-interface --td=0.25,0.5`):

        interp (M3 default, not conservative)            9.02e-3
        explode, uniform, OUT-orphan only (4.1b)         2.19e-2
        explode, uniform, both orphans                   4.72e-3
        explode, LINEAR, both orphans (this)             2.48e-3
        refine=all, no interface at all (the control)    2.29e-3

      The seam bucket is the other half of the story: it went 7.02e-2 ->
      3.33e-2 -> 3.33e-3, i.e. from six times the far field to FLAT against
      it (d=-8 3.44e-3, d=0 3.33e-3, d=+8 2.04e-3). There is no longer a
      seam signature to find.

      GATE MET, with one honest qualification. Convergence, box against its
      own no-interface control at 0.5 t_d:

        N        32        48        64
        box      4.54e-3   2.48e-3   2.11e-3
        all      4.58e-3   2.29e-3   1.98e-3

      box now tracks the CONTROL to within 8-10% at every N, which is the
      real statement: the interface is no longer the limiting error, so the
      run converges at whatever order the solver does. It is NOT
      independently demonstrated to be second order, because the control
      itself is not clean second order at these settings (ratios 2.00 and
      1.16 against 2.25 and 1.78) -- something other than the interface
      limits both, and finding it is a separate question from M4. Do not
      read the 1.18 ratio as an interface defect; the control has the same
      one.

      Conservation is untouched, as predicted: box mass drift 6.09e-1 over
      292 steps against the arithmetic floor, and `all` -- which has no
      interface -- is LARGER at 8.73e-1. Momentum still frozen.

- [x] **M4.1d — DONE 2026-09-10. The fine level integrates the body, and
      the sphere-with-AMR case exists.**

      **The blocker was never "AMR plus a body is hard", it was that the
      force reduction had no owner under a refined region.** M2 built only
      the L0 one. On the `interp` path that quietly worked, because
      `average` overwrote every covered coarse cell with restricted fine
      values for it to integrate -- at coarse resolution, but from real
      data. M4.1a partitioned the grid and M4.1b made `average` a coalesce
      that writes only the interface layer, so the coarse `f` under a
      refined region became STALE, not merely coarse. Hence the hard refusal
      in main-3d.js rather than a quietly-too-small force.

      Shipped: `common_d3_force_pool.wgsl` (+ q19/q27 entries), the
      FINEST-WINS mask in `common_d3_force.wgsl` (its own `HAS_POOL` /
      `blockSlot`, bound on every scenario with the same dummy the step
      kernel uses so the layout does not fork), and the dispatch. Two
      kernels, ONE `forces` buffer, one atomic accumulation -- a partition
      of the integral, not a second opinion on it. The restriction on
      `?interface=explode` with a body is lifted.

      **THE WEIGHT IS dx^(D-1) = dx^2 IN 3D, NOT dx^3.** Per-cell momentum
      exchange is normalized by neither cell size nor level timestep, so:
      cell mass ~ rho dx^3, dt = dx (acoustic scaling), force = mass du/dt
      -> dx^3/dx = dx^2. The 2D file records having shipped the volume
      measure alone and dropped 1/dt, costing 2x at L1; the 3D version of
      that mistake is 0.125 against 0.25, and with a body-fitted shell the
      fine level owns ALL of the body, so it is a factor of two in Cd.

      GATE MET, and the gate was CONFIRMED TO FIRE. `sphere-amr-Re20-D16`
      scores against the DENSE case's own Cd, not literature -- cross-level
      invariance is the property, and a literature comparison cannot see it
      because the staircased sphere sits +12.8% above Schiller-Naumann
      either way, which is where a weighting bug would hide. Measured:

        dense  sphere-Re20-D16            Cd 2.9436
        AMR    sphere-amr-Re20-D16        Cd 2.9416   (-0.07%)
        AMR    same, ?interface=interp    Cd 2.9621   (+0.63%)
        AMR    same, rebuilt with dx^3    Cd 1.4708   (-50.0%)

      The explode path is 10x closer to the dense reference than interp, and
      the dx^3 build misses by exactly the predicted factor of two, 100x
      over the 0.5% bound.

      ALSO ADDED: `amr-box-explode-field`, the FIELD half of the explode
      path next to `amr_conserve_cases`' conservation half. The pair is the
      point -- M4.1b was exactly conservative while carrying an O(1) seam
      defect, so neither gate implies the other.

      DEVIATION FROM THE ORIGINAL PLAN, deliberate: this stage was written
      as "new `amr-box-RB4` numbers", assuming the fix would land on the
      DEFAULT path. It did not -- `interp` is still the default, so
      amr-box-RB4 is unchanged and stays unchanged. It is now the control
      that says none of M4 disturbed the published default. The explode path
      got its own cases instead.

- [x] **M4.1-perf — explode does NOT fall off a performance cliff.**
      Measured 2026-09-10, RTX 4080, `tools/bench-d3-interface.js`. That tool
      is new and exists because the M0 spike is dense-only: it prices the
      interface at FRAME scale by differencing configurations that differ by
      one pass, which is the method
      `plans/perf-characterization.md` insists on -- per-pass GPU timestamps
      are not trustworthy at this granularity, so a pass is costed by
      REMOVING it (`?orphans=0`, `?explin=0`; both physics-wrong and there
      only to be differenced).

      | case | tiles | interp | explode | vs interp |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | `refine=body`, sphere N=16 (the realistic one) | 160 | 1.88 | 1.93 | **+2.9%** |
      | `refine=box` boxfrac=0.75, beltrami N=48 | 729 | 1.21 | 1.15 | **-4.5%** |
      | `refine=box` boxfrac=0.5, beltrami N=48 | 216 | 0.42 | 0.55 | **+31%** |

      ms per macro-step, best of N reps. **No cliff anywhere** -- the worst
      case is 1.3x, not a multiple, and the registers question sec 2.4
      settled is untouched: nothing in M4.1 added a `private` array to a hot
      kernel.

      WHERE THE COST IS, and it is one place: **the coalesce ORPHAN pass**.
      `?orphans=0` lands on interp in every geometry (-8%, -6%, -14%), so
      the explode/coalesce structure itself is FASTER than interp/average --
      `COLLIDE_RING = 0` means ring cells advect without colliding, and that
      more than pays for the coalesce. The orphan pass is what spends it.

      WHY THE SIGN FLIPS WITH GEOMETRY. The orphan pass is dispatched over
      the whole coarse grid, so its cost tracks DOMAIN size and interface
      SHELL area; the interp/average passes track refined VOLUME. Refine a
      larger fraction and explode wins; refine a small box in a small domain
      and it loses. The realistic case -- a thin body-fitted shell in a large
      domain -- lands at +2.9%, i.e. free, because there the coarse solve
      dominates both.

      **THE LINEAR EXPLOSION IS FREE.** `?explin=0` measured within noise of
      `explin=1` everywhere (0.48 vs 0.49, 0.50 vs 0.48). The six extra
      coarse loads per direction per ring cell were the obvious suspect and
      they are not the cost.

      THE OPTIMIZATION LEAD, if the +31% case ever matters: dispatch the
      orphan pass over a COMPACTED list of blocks adjacent to the refined
      region instead of over the whole coarse grid. Only a one-cell shell can
      hold an orphan (see `common_d3_amr_coalesce.wgsl`'s guard proof), so
      the useful work is a tiny fraction of the threads launched. Refinement
      is static today so the list could even be built on the CPU -- but M4.2
      makes it dynamic, and M4.2 needs a compaction pass anyway, so this is
      better done there than now.

      A NOTE ON THE MACHINE: repeat spreads of 20-95% were normal during
      this, so the tool reports the MINIMUM of the reps, not the median --
      contention only adds time. Differences under ~10% here are not
      results. That is why the guard-granularity change in the same file is
      recorded as "kept on reasoning, measured at within noise" rather than
      as a speedup.

- [x] **M4.1e — `?interface=explode` IS the default, 2026-09-10.** Everything measured
      said it should be: conservative in mass and momentum where interp is
      not, 3.6x better on the Beltrami box field, 10x closer to the dense
      sphere Cd, body-capable, and +2.9% per macro-step on the realistic
      geometry. What held it back was blast radius, not evidence, and the
      user made that call.

      **`amr-box-RB4` was NOT re-baselined onto explode**, which is a
      deliberate departure from what this item originally said. It is pinned
      to `?interface=interp` instead and keeps its old numbers (9.012e-3,
      1.024e-2). A control that tracks whatever the default happens to be is
      not a control -- pinned, it is the evidence that all of M4 left the M3
      coupling untouched. The DEFAULT path is gated by
      `amr-box-explode-field`, `amr_conserve_cases` and `sphere_amr_cases`,
      all of which name the interface explicitly for the same reason.

      ONE NUMBER MOVED, and it is worth knowing why so nobody chases it:
      `amr-all-Q27`'s decay-rate rel went -1.301e-4 -> -1.302e-4. Every
      printed field L2rel is identical at all three checkpoints and the rate
      itself still prints 1.7132e-3. With `refine=all` there is NO interface,
      so the only difference between the couplings there is that `average`
      and `coalesce` sum the eight children in different orders when they
      restrict rho and u -- same formula, and float addition is not
      associative. Q19 is bit-identical across the flip.

- [x] **M4.2a — Port the invariant checker. DONE 2026-09-10, BEFORE the
      manager, which is the whole point.** sec 7 risk #2: the 2D
      `manage`/`manage_pool` carry three separately-documented live-verified
      balance bugs, and all three sat under a green suite. A checker written
      AFTER a manager gets written to agree with it; this one is the
      independent statement of what M4.2b must achieve, written while there
      is nothing to be tempted to agree with.

      `d3-amr.mjs` gains `check21Balance` and `checkGeometryCoverage` as
      PURE functions over block sets, so `tools/test-d3-amr.js` exercises
      them with no server, browser or GPU -- **including on inputs that
      VIOLATE the invariant**, which is the only thing separating a checker
      from `return { violations: [] }`. Six new cases: a legal 3-level nest
      passes, a 3-vs-1 face gap is caught, a deeper sliver on the FAR side of
      a neighbour is correctly NOT flagged (a max-depth-anywhere checker
      would false-positive, then get loosened, then miss a real gap), the
      periodic block wrap is checked rather than clamped, a hole punched
      under the body is caught, and a body smaller than a block is caught --
      that last one is the payoff for `checkGeometryCoverage` scanning CELLS
      against the SDF instead of re-running `refineNearBody`'s own
      block-corner test, which is the thing under test rather than the
      reference.

      Worth recording: the first draft of those tests called three different
      arrangements "legal" and the checker was right about all three. A test
      whose premise is hard to get right is a test that later gets "fixed" by
      loosening the checker, so they now assert about ONE named tile on ONE
      axis rather than demanding a globally balanced tree.

      Page side: `debugCheck21Balance` / `debugCheckGeometryCoverage` on
      `window.__D3`, reading `blockSlot` BACK FROM THE GPU rather than the
      host's copy of what it uploaded -- identical today because refinement
      is static, and exactly not identical the moment M4.2b writes that
      buffer from a kernel. Runner: `tools/validate-d3-invariants.js`, a
      checkpointed sweep (a violation that appears and heals is still a
      violation, and an end-state check cannot see it).

          node tools/validate-d3-invariants.js
          node tools/validate-d3-invariants.js --steps=4000 --checkEvery=250

      GREEN, with the qualification stated in the output rather than hidden:
      **the 2:1 check is VACUOUS at `?levels=2`**. With one refined level a
      leaf's neighbour is level 1 or level 0 and both are legal, so it cannot
      fail. The tool prints that instead of a green tick, because "the
      checker ran" and "the invariant holds" are different claims. Geometry
      coverage IS a real check today and passes on `?refine=body` (4169
      cells required).

- [x] **M4.2b — Dynamic refinement + `manage`, staged. DONE 2026-09-10.** Still ordered
      AFTER the interface: dynamic refinement moves the seam and would smear
      the signal every gate above depends on.

      **SCOPE CORRECTION, and it should be said before any code is written:
      2:1 BALANCE CANNOT BE VIOLATED AT `?levels=2`.** A leaf's face
      neighbour is level 1 or level 0, and both are within one. So the "3D
      2:1 balance" half of this milestone has nothing to enforce until M5
      adds the pool-parent path and a third level. What M4.2b actually
      delivers at N=2 is DYNAMIC refinement -- allocation, initialization
      and release as the body moves. The balance cascade is written anyway,
      because writing it later means rewriting the manager rather than
      raising a level count, and 4.2a's checker prints VACUOUS until M5 so
      nobody mistakes the situation.

      Staged, each with its own gate, same discipline as M4.1:

      - [x] **4.2b-i — the pool is writable at runtime. DONE 2026-09-10.**
        `common_d3_manage.wgsl` (+ q19/q27 entries): a free-list stack, and
        SEPARATE `coarsen` and `refine` entry points dispatched as TWO
        passes. That split is not tidiness -- the 2D file records a real race
        found in a live run, where atomics serialize the free COUNTER but say
        nothing about when one thread's plain write to `freeList` becomes
        visible to another's plain read in the same dispatch, and two coarse
        blocks ended up pointing at one slot. Opt-in (`?dynamic=1`), refused
        unless `?refine=body`, since a fixed region has no criterion worth
        re-deciding. The pool now allocates `?slotHeadroom=` (default 1.5x)
        above the initial set, because a manager that can only hand back what
        it already has is not a manager.

        GATE MET: `sphere-amr-dynamic-Re20-D16` is BIT-IDENTICAL to
        `sphere-amr-Re20-D16` (Cd 2.9416, exact equality, not a tolerance --
        `cd_matches_exact`). Same shape as M4.1a's gate and for the same
        reason: introduce the machinery against an unchanged answer, then
        make it load-bearing.

        **BUT THAT GATE CANNOT SEE WHETHER THE MANAGER RAN**, and saying so
        is half the value of this stage. "Nothing changed" is equally
        consistent with a kernel that never executed. Two things came out of
        taking that seriously:

        - A REAL BUG, found by reading rather than by testing: the
          re-evaluation interval tested `step % MANAGE_EVERY`, and `step`
          only advances after a whole batch (`debugStepSync` chunks 500 at a
          time), so the manager ran on all 500 steps of one chunk and none of
          the next. With a pinned body that is indistinguishable from
          correct. Now `(step + s)`.
        - `debugPoolState()` and two allocator-exercise configs in
          `tools/validate-d3-invariants.js`. `?manageMargin=` gives the
          MANAGER a criterion that deliberately disagrees with the initial
          set, so the pool has to move, and the tool asserts it does:

              body-dynamic   inUse 160 -> 160   (no-op, as designed)
              body-coarsen   inUse 160 ->  88   free  80 -> 152
              body-refine    inUse 160 -> 240   free  80 ->   0

          with `blockSlot` and `slotToBlock` mutual inverses and the budget
          balancing at every checkpoint throughout -- the cross-check that
          CONFIRMED the 2D race, since they disagreed for exactly the
          colliding slot. `body-refine` also saturates the headroom, so it
          exercises the out-of-slots path: the pop restores the counter and
          leaves the block coarse rather than corrupting the list.

        `body-refine` BLOWS THE FIELD UP, expectedly and gated as such: it
        hands out tiles nothing has initialized. That is 4.2b-ii, and this is
        the evidence for why it is next rather than optional. When it lands,
        the `fieldWrongByDesign` flag comes off and the blowup must stop.

      - [x] **4.2b-ii — newly-refined tiles are filled, released ones are
        restricted. DONE 2026-09-10.**

        **A CORRECTION FIRST: the note this stage was written with was
        wrong.** It said reaching for `average` would be a mistake because
        Chen's scheme wants no Dupuis-Chopard rescale. That reasoning does
        not transfer. Chen's no-rescale is a statement about the INTERFACE,
        where one state is moved between two bookkeepings of a single volume
        and the a = (n-1)/2n offset absorbs the tau difference. A tile being
        created or destroyed is an ordinary GRID TRANSFER: the two grids must
        represent the same rho, u and viscous stress, fneq carries the
        stress, and it scales by (tau_f - 1)/(tau_c - 1)/2. So `interp` and
        `average` are exactly right, rescale included -- a new tile without
        it would be born with the wrong stress.

        Implemented as THIRD PIPELINES over the two existing modules, not new
        shaders: `interp` gains `NEW_ONLY`, `average` gains `DYING_ONLY`. A
        tile being born wants precisely interp's transfer and one being
        absorbed wants precisely average's restriction, and a second copy of
        either is how the two would drift.

        **FIVE PASSES, AND THE ORDER IS THE DESIGN:**

            decide   evaluate the criterion ONCE into blockWant. 4.2b-i
                     evaluated it separately in coarsen and refine -- two
                     chances to disagree about one question, four once drain
                     and fill also need the answer.
            drain    restrict a dying tile onto its coarse cells. MUST
                     precede coarsen: coarsen frees the slot and refine can
                     hand that same slot out in the very next pass, by which
                     point the fine solution is gone.
            coarsen  free the slots.
            refine   allocate, and mark the slot new.
            fill     initialize the just-allocated tiles. MUST follow refine
                     -- there is no slot to fill until one is handed out.
            clear    drop the new-flags, in ITS OWN PASS.

        That last split is a race that was written and then caught: clearing
        the flag inside `fill` means one thread clears the very value its
        siblings use to decide whether to do their work, and threads in a
        dispatch are not ordered -- so a tile would be filled in part and
        left in part as whatever the previous owner had. There is no "last"
        thread to do it from.

        GATE MET: `validate-d3-invariants.js`'s `body-refine` config
        allocates 80 tiles and NO LONGER BLOWS UP -- the
        `fieldWrongByDesign` flag is gone, which is exactly the gate 4.2b-i
        set up. All six configs PASS.

        THE DRAIN, MEASURED rather than assumed. `?manageStart=` (new, and
        it exists for this) places a single coarsening event at step 300, so
        the flow has developed and the fine solution genuinely differs from
        the coarse one -- at step 0 the two still hold the same initial
        condition and a drain would be indistinguishable from doing nothing.
        Mass discontinuity across that event, 160 tiles -> 88:

            without drain   -1.615e+1
            with drain      -1.334e+1

        so the drain removes ~17% of it. The residual is not a defect in the
        drain: `average`'s rho is the arithmetic mean of the eight children,
        which is exactly mass-conserving, and what remains is the real
        physics of de-refining a body's OWN shell -- the bounce-back surface
        stops being resolved. That configuration is violent by construction
        and only arises because `?manageMargin=` is deliberately wrong; the
        geometry criterion never does it in normal operation. Both figures
        are ~4e-6 of the domain's mass.

      - [x] **4.2b-iii — geometry-forced refinement follows a MOVING body.
        DONE 2026-09-10.** The one stage 4.2a's checker can genuinely gate,
        because its coverage half is real where the 2:1 half is vacuous.

        Needed a body that TRANSLATES, and nothing in the suite had one --
        `sphere` is pinned and `spin` turns in place, which never moves a
        shell across a block boundary. New `drift` scenario: a free sphere
        with a constant velocity and NO_FLUID_FORCE, so its trajectory is
        exactly x0 + v t and a coverage failure is the manager rather than
        the flow. Added to `d3-scenarios.mjs`, not as a page -- sec 5 M1's
        decision.

        Also fixed: `debugCheckGeometryCoverage` was testing against the
        body's INITIAL position, held in a closure since startup. Against a
        moving body that would pass a manager which refined a shell once and
        then left it behind -- the exact failure being gated. It now reads
        the LIVE body back from the GPU, which is the only place the truth
        lives, since the physics kernel steps the body and the host does not.

        **THE GATE FAILED FIRST, AND THE FAILURE WAS REAL.** Coverage broke
        at two checkpoints, always at cells sitting exactly on the margin
        boundary. Cause: the criterion is re-evaluated every MANAGE_EVERY
        steps, so a shell that only just covers the body AT DECISION TIME is
        already stale on the next step -- and the geometry-forced constraint
        is supposed to hold at every step, not only the ones the manager runs
        on. The body had moved MANAGE_EVERY * |v| = 0.08 cells since the
        decision. Tiny, and enough, because a boundary is a boundary.

        Fix: the criterion REFINES AHEAD, `reach = MARGIN + MANAGE_EVERY *
        |v|`. It is exactly zero for a pinned body, so M4.2b-i's
        bit-identical gate is untouched. Rotation is deliberately not in the
        term -- it matters for a long body spun about a short axis, and every
        body using `?refine=body` today is a sphere, for which it is zero;
        an untested term in a criterion whose job is to be checkable is worse
        than an absent one.

        CONFIRMED LOAD-BEARING: rebuilt with the lead term forced to zero,
        the same two checkpoints fail again. The threshold change made in the
        same pass (`expectBboxMove` 8 -> 4, because the body only travels 6
        blocks in 1200 steps at u0 = 0.02) is NOT what made it green.

        The other half of the gate is that the shell actually MOVED:
        `inUse` cannot show it, since a translating sphere refines as many
        blocks ahead as it coarsens behind and the count stays at 160 while
        every tile changes hands. `debugPoolState` now reports the refined
        set's bounding box, and the config requires its leading edge to
        advance.

      - [x] **4.2b-iv — the 2:1 cascade. DONE 2026-09-10, and the shipped
        artifact is a HOST function plus an absent kernel.** The rule is
        `d3-amr.mjs`'s `cascade21`; `shaders/common_d3_manage.wgsl` has no
        `balance` entry point, and its header says why rather than leaving
        the gap to be read as an oversight.

        **BOTH HALVES OF THIS ITEM ARE ONE CLOSURE**, which is why it is one
        function and not two tests bolted onto coarsen and refine:

            present(m, b)  =>  present(m-1, parent(n))   for each face
                               neighbour n of b at level m, and for b itself

        Read forwards that is "refine forced by a neighbour that wants a
        deeper child"; read backwards -- a block the criterion did NOT want,
        added back -- it is "coarsen blocked by a neighbour that has one".
        It runs on the WANT set, between `decide` and `drain`, so by the
        time coarsen and refine execute the answer is already balanced and
        neither needs to know anything about levels. The 2D manager reached
        the same place by a different road and carries three separately
        documented live-verified balance bugs from doing it per-pass.

        **WHY NO KERNEL, and this is the deliverable rather than a shortcut.**
        The closure is the IDENTITY when there is one refined level: a
        level-1 block's parent level is the dense L0 grid, which is present
        everywhere, so there is nothing to require. A `balance` pass at
        ?levels=2 could only be a dispatch that writes nothing back --
        exactly the shape sec 7 risk #2 warns about, code that has never
        been run against an input that could make it fail. So the rule went
        where it CAN be gated today: `tools/test-d3-amr.js`, seven checks in
        `make test`, no GPU.

        SCORED AGAINST THE CHECKER, NOT AGAINST ITSELF. `check21Balance` was
        written in M4.2a, deliberately before there was a manager to agree
        with; the cascade proposes a balanced set and that checker -- which
        knows nothing about the closure rule -- either finds a gap or does
        not. What is asserted:

            identity at levels=2      20 random one-level sets, unchanged.
                                      This is the check on "provably".
            closes a real 3-vs-1 gap  the checker reports it, the cascade
                                      removes it, the checker then reports
                                      nothing.
            a REAL nested shell       three independently-evaluated geometry
                                      shells round a sphere -- the shape M5
                                      will produce, and unbalanced by
                                      construction because no per-level
                                      distance test knows about the level
                                      above it. 88/272/1680 wanted ->
                                      184/704/2432 balanced, recorded.
            idempotent                the single deepest-first sweep IS a
                                      fixed point, checked not argued.
            minimality                removing any forced OCTET leaves a
                                      tree that violates 2:1 or orphans a
                                      sub-tree. Without this, "refine a halo
                                      to be safe" passes everything else --
                                      a superset of a balanced set is still
                                      balanced.

        **THE BUG THE FIRST DRAFT HAD, because it is the one M5 will hit
        again: REFINEMENT IS OCTET-COMPLETE FROM LEVEL 2 DOWN.** A parent
        spawns all eight children or none -- `amr_manage_pool.wgsl` says it
        outright for the 2D quad, and there is no such thing as a
        quarter-refined parent when a tile is allocated per block.
        `check21Balance` leans on it too: `hasChild` tests octant (0,0,0)
        alone. The first cascade added single blocks, so a parent holding
        only octant (1,0,0) still read as a LEAF and the checker reported
        the very violation the cascade was supposed to have removed. The
        checker was right. A want for one child is a want for the parent's
        refinement, so the input is completed to octets rather than
        rejected.

        GATE: `make test` 25 checks, and `validate-d3-invariants.js` all
        seven configs PASS unchanged (the manager's GPU path is untouched by
        this stage -- the WGSL and `main-3d.js` edits are comments).
        4.2a still prints VACUOUS, correctly: dynamic refinement moves tiles
        WITHIN one level, so nothing in M4.2b can make the check non-vacuous.
        M5 is what does, and if it lands without that line changing, either
        the manager is not building multi-level trees or the checker is not
        seeing what it writes.

      Note from M4.1-perf: this needs a compaction pass (active blocks -> a
      dispatch list) anyway, and the orphan pass's whole-grid dispatch is the
      one place explode costs more than interp. Build the compaction once and
      use it for both.

**Superseded by M4.1 and kept only as the record**: the flux-correction
("reflux") pass, `?reflux=1`. It is what measured the corner and is why the
scheme above was found; do not extend it.

**M5 — N=3 (pool-parent path), `S_Advance` recursion at depth. DONE
2026-09-10, M5.0 through M5.6.** `?levels=N` runs at any depth, static or
`?dynamic=1`; the body lives entirely on the finest level; 2:1 balance and
ring-parent coverage are real gates rather than VACUOUS lines; and the
depth-3 field, conservation, Cd and manager claims are all in
`benchmarks/d3.json`.
Mechanically the 2D M5-M10 arc (`plans/AMR-multilevel.md`), and the
recursion itself is verbatim. Staged below, each stage with its own gate,
the same discipline M4 used -- this is the largest remaining milestone and
"port the 2D arc" is not a plan.

**TWO THINGS ARE ALREADY TRUE HERE THAT WERE NOT TRUE IN 2D**, and both
should be checked before any of the 2D plan is copied:

- **The tile shape is ALREADY octree-uniform, so there is no topology
  change to make.** 2D's M5 had to convert a footprint-preserving
  1-parent-to-1-double-density-tile scheme into a quadtree. 3D does not:
  a tile's interior is 2*RB cells at its OWN level, which spans
  RB * 2^(1-m) L0 units, so a level-m tile is exactly one OCTANT of a
  level-(m-1) tile's footprint at doubled density. Same FB, same kernels,
  same everything -- the level-2 tile is the level-1 tile moved down a
  rung. `nbAt(m) = nb(1) * 2^(m-1)`, which is precisely what
  `check21Balance` and `cascade21` were already written against in M4.2a
  and M4.2b-iv.
- **The 2:1 forcing rule already exists and is tested** -- `cascade21`,
  M4.2b-iv. M5 does not design it; M5 mirrors it into a kernel and turns
  `validate-d3-invariants.js`'s VACUOUS line off.

Also inherited from the 2D arc and NOT to be re-derived: the two addressing
schemes coexist rather than unify (L0->L1 reads the dense buffer spatially;
L(m)->L(m+1) reads a parent TILE through a stored `parentSlot`), the
allocator works in OCTET units and never fragments below 8 (M4.2b-iv's
decision-table row), and a tile's own logical block coordinates at its level
are STORED at allocation, not re-derived by walking the parent chain on
every dispatch.

- [x] **M5.0 — `?levels>=3` is REFUSED, loudly. DONE 2026-09-10.** It is accepted today and
      silently runs TWO levels, because `AMR = LEVELS >= 2` and exactly one
      pool is allocated. Worse, `debugCheck21Balance` reports
      `vacuous: LEVELS < 3`, so at `?levels=3` the one tool that would
      notice announces it is checking a hierarchy that does not exist.
      Every stage below could then look green while doing nothing, which is
      the failure mode this whole milestone has to be defended against.
      Refuse it at init until the pool-parent path exists, and lift the
      refusal in M5.3, when there is something to run.

      REFUSED RATHER THAN CLAMPED, because clamping to 2 is the same silence
      with a different spelling: a validation config written as `?levels=3`
      would pass and mean nothing. VERIFIED against the page: `?levels=3`
      and `?levels=4` reach `error: ?levels=N is not implemented yet` with
      no `window.__D3`, `?levels=2` boots unchanged (160/49152 blocks).

- [x] **M5.1 — the layout, in two halves so the host part is testable
      before the GPU part is refactored. DONE (5.1a/b/c).**

      - [x] **5.1a — host: the octree addressing at depth. DONE 2026-09-10**
        (`d3-amr.mjs`: `poolAtLevel`, `parentOfBlock`, `octantOfBlock`,
        `octantOrigin`).
        A per-level VIEW of the same pool -- `{ rb, GHOST, FB, nb: nbAt(m) }`
        -- so `resolveSource` and the global-fine route work unchanged at any
        level, which is the concrete payoff of the uniform tile shape above.
        Plus what a pool parent needs and a dense parent does not:
        `parentOf(b) = b >> 1`, `octantOf(b) = b & 1`, and the child's
        position inside the parent's interior, which falls out of the octant
        bits alone: offset `GHOST + q_a * RB` on axis a, because the parent's
        2*RB interior cells are halved by the octant. The SAME
        `fineToCoarseUnit` then maps a child-local index into parent-local
        continuous coordinates with that offset as its `origin` -- one
        function, two callers, not a second copy of the stencil.
        GATE MET: `tools/test-d3-amr.js`, three new checks, no GPU.
        `resolveSource` agrees with the INDEPENDENT global-coordinate route
        at level 2 AND level 3 -- ownership by one division, no ring, no
        offsets -- and the octant placement is checked the same way: child
        global cell -> parent global cell by one halving -> parent block by
        one division -> parent-local index, a route sharing nothing with
        `octantOrigin`'s closed form. The CONTINUOUS mapping is checked
        too, against physical positions, since that is what the
        interpolation stencil actually consumes.

        `poolAtLevel` turned out to be a ONE-LINER -- level m's tiling is
        `makePool` on a domain scaled by 2^(m-1) -- which is the uniform
        tile shape stated as code rather than as a claim. It is also why
        `resolveSource`, `toGlobalFine`, `fromGlobalFine` and `cellIndex`
        need no change at depth at all.

        MUTATION-CHECKED, because a test that agrees with a closed form it
        was written alongside proves nothing: an off-by-one in the octant
        offset, a flipped octant, and a transposed `parentOfBlock` each fail
        two checks. A transposed table is the highest-consequence,
        lowest-visibility bug available in this port (sec 6 item 3).

      - [x] **5.1b — host: the refinement HIERARCHY. DONE 2026-09-10**
        (`d3-amr.mjs`'s `refineHierarchy`). Split out of what was one stage,
        because the buffer sizes 5.1c allocates depend on an answer nobody
        had yet: what IS level 2's refinement set?

        **THE CRITERION IS EVALUATED AT THE FINEST LEVEL ONLY**, and every
        coarser level is whatever `cascade21` requires around it. That is
        the correct statement of what sec 1.3 asks for -- push the body's
        surface onto the FINEST configured level -- and the intermediate
        shells exist solely as the buffer 2:1 balance demands, which is
        exactly what the closure produces. M4.2b-iv's minimality test is
        the proof there is no halo of unnecessary tiles.

        It also DISPOSES OF A WORRY THE 2D ARC CARRIED INTO ITS M10:
        "retune the thresholds per level, a level-2 region at the surface
        with level-1 as the buffer shell 2:1 balance requires". For a
        geometry-forced criterion there is nothing per level to tune --
        there is one margin, at the finest level, and the shells follow. A
        vorticity-driven criterion will need per-level thresholds; this one
        does not, and that should not have to be rediscovered.

        GATE MET, and it is the same shape as M4.1a's: at `levels=2` the
        hierarchy is BIT-IDENTICAL to `refineWhere`/`refineNearBody` --
        asserted on four predicates including the real geometry-forced one,
        not argued from "the closure is the identity there". At depth 3 and
        4 the tree is 2:1 balanced by `check21Balance` (the M4.2a checker,
        not the closure that built it), properly parented, each coarser
        level ENCLOSES the finer one in physical units, and the body is
        covered at CELL granularity at the finest level -- driven at depth
        3 where the finest view's cells are 4x smaller than an L0 cell, so
        a wrong L0 rescale would build a shell of the wrong physical size
        and this is what says so.

        **MEASURED, and 5.1c needs it: THE LEVEL-1 TILE COUNT IS NOT
        INDEPENDENT OF THE DEPTH.** On the `sphere-amr-Re20-D16` geometry
        (192x128x128, RB=4, D=16, margin 2):

            levels=2   L1=160                       40 MiB of f-pool
            levels=3   L1=304  L2=1280             397 MiB
            levels=4   L1=376  L2=1664  L3=6336   2098 MiB

        L1 grows 160 -> 304 -> 376 because a deeper level demands a wider
        buffer shell around it. So a per-level budget CANNOT be derived
        from the `levels=2` answer, and `MAX_SLOTS` has to come from the
        hierarchy itself. Device limits measured on the RTX 4080 the same
        day: `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` 2 GiB, `maxBufferSize` 4 GiB,
        `maxStorageBuffersPerShaderStage` 16, `maxBindGroups` 4 -- so
        levels=3 is comfortable and levels=4's largest single binding
        (832 MiB) still fits, which makes VRAM rather than the binding
        limit the ceiling. The 16-buffer figure is the one to watch in
        M5.2: a pool-parent kernel binds both levels.

      - [x] **5.1c — `main-3d.js` consumes it: per-level buffer arrays.
        DONE 2026-09-10.** Every
        pool buffer (`fPoolA/B`, `macPool`, `blockSlot`, `slotToBlock`,
        `freeList`/`freeCount`, `blockWant`, `slotNew`) becomes `L[m].*`,
        allocated by a loop over levels and sized from `refineHierarchy`'s
        own per-level counts, never from the depth-2 answer. Same
        bind-group LAYOUTS and pipeline objects across levels -- only the
        bindings and a per-level uniform differ, which is sec 6's
        anti-238e48c rule applied to levels instead of to pages.
        GATE MET: `?levels=2` is BIT-IDENTICAL. `validate-3d.js` all 32
        cases, every printed figure unchanged to the last digit;
        `validate-d3-invariants.js` all seven configs with identical tile
        counts. M4.1a's gate shape and for M4.1a's reason: introduce the
        machinery against an unchanged answer, then make it load-bearing.

        WHAT MOVED, and it is more than allocation:

        - `makeLevelBuffers(m)` allocates one level; `L[m]` holds it. The
          depth-2 dispatch still addresses level 1 through named ALIASES
          rather than a rename across the file -- M5.3's recursive schedule
          is what replaces those, and doing it now would be a large diff
          that no gate could distinguish from a correct one.
        - `debugCheck21Balance` now reads EVERY level's map back from the
          GPU rather than level 1's. This is the one piece that had to
          change ahead of need: a checker fed one level of a three-level
          tree cannot fail, which is the same VACUOUS trap M5.0 refused. It
          is a no-op at depth 2 and correct at depth 3 the day there is one.
        - `debugPoolState` reports `byLevel` and keeps its level-1 fields at
          the top, so `validate-d3-invariants.js` reads exactly what it read
          before. Same for `getParams`, which gains `levelTiles`.
        - The two readback staging buffers are sized from the DEEPEST level,
          not level 1. The block count doubles per axis per level, so level
          1's size is 8x short at level 2 -- a readback that silently
          returns a prefix, and a checker that then reports a perfectly
          balanced tree because it never saw the rest.

        DELIBERATELY NOT DONE HERE: per-level BIND GROUPS. There is one
        layout and one pipeline set already, which is the part of "same
        layouts across levels" that is a design claim; building a second
        level's bind groups with no second level to bind is the untestable-
        code argument M4.2b-iv and M5.2 both make. They land in M5.2, where
        a pool-parent pipeline actually needs them.

- [x] **M5.2a — RING PARENT COVERAGE: the invariant the pool parent needs,
      and the closure change it forced. DONE 2026-09-10.** Found by reading
      `common_d3_amr_step1.wgsl` before writing the kernel, which is the only
      way it could have been found: the configuration that exercises it
      cannot exist until `?levels=3`.

      THE REQUIREMENT. A tile's ring extends one PARENT cell beyond its block
      on every one of the 26 sides, and `explode` fills a ring cell from the
      parent cell containing it. At level 1 that is free -- the parent is the
      dense L0 grid, which exists everywhere. At level >= 2 the parent is a
      POOL, and a tile that is not there cannot be read from.

      **2:1 BALANCE DOES NOT IMPLY IT.** Child block b = (2p, 2p, z), lower
      octant on x and y. Its diagonal neighbour (2p-1, 2p-1, z) has parent
      (p-1, p-1); the face closure supplies (p-1, p) and (p, p-1) and NOT
      (p-1, p-1). And that cell IS read: `step1` resolves a source against
      the diagonal same-level neighbour tile (`nbrXYZ`) and falls back to the
      RING when that tile is absent -- exactly the configuration where the
      corner ring cell matters. `explode` skips ring cells inside a COVERED
      parent cell, so the cells it writes are precisely the ones the step
      reads: the two agree, and both need the parent.

      So `cascade21`'s closure went from the SIX FACES to all 27 offsets --
      d = (0,0,0) is the tree property, the faces are 2:1 balance, and the
      twelve edges and eight corners are the ring.

      NOT the "corner-balance refinement constraint" sec 2.1 cites as a cost
      of ghost-free: that one is about SAME-LEVEL corner neighbours. This is
      about the PARENT level, and it is what keeping the ring costs at depth.

      MEASURED, and it is cheap. Sphere geometry, 26-closure vs face-only:

          levels=3   L1 304 -> 432   L2 1280 -> 1280   397 -> 429 MiB  (+8%)
          levels=4   L1 376 -> 600   L2 1664 -> 1856   2098 -> 2202 MiB (+5%)

      The FINEST level never moves, which is the sanity check on the claim:
      the closure only ever adds coarser tiles.

      GATE: `checkRingParentCoverage` is its own checker, not a clause in
      `check21Balance`, because they are different claims -- and the test
      proves it by building a tree where they DISAGREE: 2:1 balance is
      silent on a block whose corner parent is missing, and the ring check
      is not. A closure gated on `check21Balance` alone would have shipped
      the face-only version. Wired into the page (`debugCheckRingParents`)
      and into `validate-d3-invariants.js` as its own column, reporting
      VACUOUS at `?levels=2` for its own reason -- level 1's parent is dense
      -- rather than borrowing the 2:1 line's. All seven configs PASS with
      unchanged tile counts; `make test` 32 checks.

- [x] **M5.2b-i — the PARENT ADDRESS, abstracted; dense implementation
      only. DONE 2026-09-10.** `shaders/common_d3_parent_dense.wgsl`, and
      `explode`/`coalesce` now go through `parentIndex(v)` and a stride taken
      from the binding rather than `coarseCell(v)` and `NX*NY*NZ`.
      M4.1a's shape again: introduce the machinery against an unchanged
      answer.

      **THE GOOD NEWS, and it should be checked before the 2D arc's M6 is
      copied: almost nothing in `common_d3_pool.wgsl` is L0-specific.** Read
      `NX/NY/NZ` as "the PARENT grid's dimensions" instead of "L0's" and
      `nbx() = NX/RB` is that level's block count, `fineDim() = 2*NX` its
      child-cell count, and `blockOfCoarse`, `coarseOfFine`, `blockOfFine`
      and `localInTile3` all follow unchanged. That is the uniform tile
      shape paying off in the kernels exactly as `poolAtLevel` did on the
      host. The ONE genuinely different thing is how a parent CELL is
      addressed in the parent's `f` array -- a dense index, or a tile lookup
      through the parent's own `blockSlot` -- so that is all the two parent
      fragments provide.

      Also folded in: the linear explosion's per-axis admissibility test now
      requires the two neighbours to EXIST as well as be unrefined. M5.2a's
      ring-parent invariant covers the cells explode WRITES, dilated by one
      parent cell; the central difference reaches one further. Widening the
      invariant would cost tiles on every seam to buy a second-order term at
      a few of them, so instead the explosion degrades to UNIFORM there --
      the path it already takes at a concave corner, first-order and exactly
      conservative, so it cannot move a conservation gate. `parentPresent`
      is constant-true for a dense parent and the guard folds away.

      GATE MET: bit-identical at `?levels=2`. `validate-3d.js`'s AMR and
      sphere cases print every figure unchanged (`amr-box-RB4` 9.012e-3,
      `amr-bar-explode` d mass 5.682e-1 / d mom 3.015e-4,
      `sphere-amr` Cd 2.9416).

      **FOUND WHILE READING, and M5.2b-ii has to deal with it: `coalesce`
      DISPATCHES OVER THE DENSE GRID** (`if (gid.x >= NX ...) return;`), one
      thread per coarse cell. A pool parent is not a grid, so at depth the
      dispatch has to walk parent TILES -- `(FB/4, FB/4, (FB/4) * parentSlots)`
      with the parent cell derived from (slot, local), interior only. The
      BODY is unchanged; only the way `c` is obtained differs, which is why
      it splits the same way `parentIndex` did rather than forking the
      physics. The orphan pass comes along for free: "unrefined parent cells
      near the seam" are exactly the interior cells of parent tiles that no
      child covers.

- [x] **M5.2b-ii — the pool parent itself, driven BY HAND. DONE
      2026-09-10** (the momentum gate it left red is closed by M5.2b-iii). A depth-3 run is stable,
      mass-conserving and tracks its no-interface control on the field. It
      LEAKS MOMENTUM at the L1/L2 seam, the gate says so, and that is the
      open item -- recorded here rather than left as a green tick, because
      `amr-box-explode-L3` PASSES and only `amr-bar-explode-L3` fails, which
      is exactly the shape that gets mistaken for noise.

      WHAT LANDED:
      - `common_d3_parent_pool.wgsl`, the sibling of M5.2b-i's dense
        fragment, plus pool-parent entry files for explode, coalesce and
        interp. One copy of the physics, two parent addresses.
      - `coalesce`'s dispatch split (M5.2b-i's finding): `coalesceAt(c)` is
        shared and the two `main`s differ only in how `c` is obtained --
        a dense grid, or a walk over parent TILES, interior only.
      - Per-level pipelines and bind groups, per-level tau
        (`tauAtLevel`), and the hand-flattened depth-3 stanza in
        `encodeSteps`: each of L1's two substeps wraps a whole L2
        macro-step, with the parent's own substep LAST, which is the
        depth-2 shape verbatim.
      - `?handdepth=1`, without which `?levels=3` is still refused. It also
        forces `live` off: nothing SCHEDULES the hierarchy until M5.3, so a
        live animation would present a hand-flattened stanza as the solver.
      - `?handskip=explode2,step2,coalesce2`, a bisection hook. It is what
        found the seeding bug below, in one run.
      - Depth-3 rows in `analyze-d3-interface.js` and two depth-3
        conservation cases in `benchmarks/d3.json`, N=32.

      **THE BUG THE BISECTION FOUND, and it is worth the hook's existence:
      a new level's pool is NEVER SEEDED.** `reset()` fills level 1 from the
      L0 initial condition with `interpFullPipe` and stops. Level 2 held
      whatever the GPU zeroed it to, and zero is not a fluid state -- rho = 0
      there, so the first coalesce handed L1 nothing and the run detonated in
      ONE step. What identified it was `?handskip=explode2,step2`: with L2
      never stepped at all, coalesce ALONE still blew L1 up, which points at
      L2's CONTENTS and not at the pass. Fixed by cascading the seed down
      every level in order, each from its own parent, with the pool-parent
      interp.

      **THE OPEN DEFECT: momentum at the L1/L2 seam.** Measured N=32,
      warmup-relative (the same measure the depth-2 rows use, and for the
      same reason -- there is a real one-time startup offset):

          case                 d mass            d mom      bound
          amr-bar-explode      5.682e-1        3.015e-4     5.0 / 5e-2
          amr-box-explode      5.306e-1        2.112e-4
          amr-bar-explode-L3   2.057e-1        1.224e-1     FAILS
          amr-box-explode-L3   1.730e-1        3.467e-2     passes, 160x

      MASS IS FINE -- better than depth 2, and below the `all3` control's own
      f32 floor. It is the FIRST MOMENT that leaks, and `box3` passing while
      `bar3` fails is not evidence of a corner: `bar` spans the full z axis
      and simply has more seam.

      **THE LEAD, and the experiment that sharpened it.** At depth 2 the
      DENSE step skips cells covered by a finer level (`coveredByFiner`) --
      Chen's scheme removes a covered cell from the coarser level's
      dynamics. `common_d3_amr_step1.wgsl` has no such check, so at depth 3
      L1 keeps solving where L2 covers it, and a covered L1 cell ends up
      holding a MIX: the directions coalesce wrote, and the directions L1's
      own step produced. That mixture is neither level's solution, and its
      moments are what `coalesce(L1 -> L0)` hands upward.

      Adding the obvious skip MADE IT FAR WORSE -- mass drift -8.0e+3 -- and
      the reason is the useful half. `coalesce(L1 -> L0)` recomputes a
      covered cell's moments from L1's POPULATIONS, and coalesce only ever
      writes the directions whose target is unrefined; the rest are left
      because at depth 2 nobody reads them. Skip the step and those
      directions are never written by anything, so the population vector is
      not a distribution at all. So the skip is not wrong, it is
      INCOMPLETE: a covered parent cell needs a COMPLETE state, which means
      either coalesce writing all QN directions there (the straight average
      of the eight children, which is what `average` already computes), or
      `coalesce(L1 -> L0)` taking a covered cell's moments from the parent's
      macPool, which `coalesce(L2 -> L1)` already maintains correctly. The
      first is the smaller change and the more likely right one. That is
      M5.2b-iii.

- [x] **M5.2b-iii — the depth-3 momentum leak. DONE 2026-09-10, and it was
      TWO defects, one of which was hiding the other.**

      **THE REAL ONE: `mac` AND `mac_pool` HAVE DIFFERENT MEMORY LAYOUTS.**
      The dense L0 `mac` is INTERLEAVED, `[rho,ux,uy,uz]` per cell, because
      the renderer and every host readback walk it that way. A pool
      `mac_pool` is PLANAR, component-major, like its `f` array. At depth 3
      `coalesce(L2 -> L1)` binds L1's macPool to the `mac` slot and was
      writing it in the DENSE layout, so every moment landed in a different
      cell. It survived a first look because both are `array<f32>` of
      exactly the right length -- the binding is valid, the writes are in
      range, and nothing anywhere says which convention applies.

      Fixed by making the layout part of the parent abstraction:
      `parentMacIndex(cell, comp, plane)`, dense and pool, alongside
      `parentIndex`. Same shape as M5.2b-i.

      **THE OTHER ONE, which was real and was being masked: `step1` did not
      skip cells covered by a finer level.** `coveredByFiner` is a thing the
      DENSE step has always had; the pool step never needed it because
      nothing was ever finer than the fine level. At depth 3, coalesce
      writes the parent's `mac_pool` at covered cells correctly and then
      `step1` ran afterwards and overwrote it -- with moments of a
      population vector that is part delivery-OUTBOX and part self-stepped.
      A covered cell's populations are not a distribution by design; taking
      moments of one is the mistake.

      INTERIOR ONLY, and the first attempt got this wrong: it skipped RING
      cells too, on the reasoning that a covered ring cell is never read. A
      ring cell is this level's ghost for ITS OWN parent and the parent's
      coalesce harvests the outflux out of exactly those cells, so it must
      keep advecting whether or not a child covers it. The two roles are
      independent and one cell serves both.

      **THE ORDER THEY WERE FOUND IN IS THE LESSON.** The skip alone made
      mass drift go from 2.0e-1 to -8.0e+3, which reads as "the skip is
      wrong" -- and the skip is right. It got worse because removing
      `step1`'s overwrite let the mis-laid-out moments SURVIVE instead of
      being partly papered over. A change that makes a number worse can be
      unmasking rather than causing, which is the mirror of M4.1c's
      "a stage that measures as a no-op may be downstream of something
      bigger".

      GATE MET:

          case                 d mass       d mom      was
          amr-bar-explode-L3   2.003e-1   2.938e-4     1.224e-1  FAIL
          amr-box-explode-L3   1.704e-1   1.949e-4     3.467e-2

      Both now at the depth-2 rows' own order (3.015e-4 / 2.112e-4), i.e.
      the L1/L2 seam is as conservative as the L0/L1 one. `validate-3d.js`
      all 34 cases PASS with every depth-2 figure bit-identical.

      **AND THE CLASS IS CLOSED, not just the instance.** The layout now
      lives in the TYPE: the dense `mac` is `array<vec4<f32>>` and a pool's
      is `array<f32>`, declared by `common_d3_parentmac_{dense,pool}.wgsl`,
      and the shared coalesce body no longer declares the array at all --
      it cannot name it, so it cannot express either layout, only call
      `parentMacStore`. That is the part that actually prevents the bug;
      the vec4 type is the second line, making a mistake INSIDE a fragment
      a compile error.

      MEASURED IN CHROME on the 4080 before committing to it, because naga
      accepting a thing is not Tint accepting it and neither is it being
      free:
      - the binding and a DYNAMIC component index (`mac[i][k]`, the render
        shader's pattern) both compile and run correctly;
      - `vec4<f32>` is four consecutive floats with NO padding, so the bytes
        are identical to `4*cell+c` and every host readback and bind-group
        layout is untouched;
      - min of 6 alternating rounds of 200 dispatches over 2^20 cells: vec4
        at 0.953x the scalar version, inside this desktop's noise floor.
      Verified by screenshot as well as by number -- the renderer is the one
      consumer whose failure mode is visual, not numeric.

      WHAT IT DOES NOT CATCH, so it is not over-claimed: a wrong CELL index
      is still a `u32`, and the bind-group layout cannot tell the two
      buffers apart on the JS side (both are `buffer: {type:'storage'}`), so
      binding the wrong buffer from `main-3d.js` stays uncaught. Also worth
      recording: **`const_assert` is rejected on `override` expressions**
      (checked, naga: "Unexpected override-expression"), and nearly all
      shape information here lives in overrides -- so static assertions
      about shapes are not available in WGSL at all, which is why the
      type-level route is the only one with teeth. Explode/coalesce with
      a POOL parent, plus `step1` and `average` at depth, dispatched from a
      debug entry point rather than by the scheduler. That ordering is
      deliberate and it is the same argument M4.2b-iv made about the balance
      kernel: a scheduler's depth-3 branch written before there is anything
      for it to dispatch is code that has never run against an input that
      could make it fail.

      THE PIECE THAT IS GENUINELY NEW, and it is not in the 2D arc at all:
      3D's interface is Chen explode/coalesce and it is EXACTLY
      CONSERVATIVE (M4.1). 2D's M6 generalizes interp/average, which 3D
      keeps only as the `?interface=interp` A/B path. So the pool-parent
      work is on `common_d3_amr_explode.wgsl` / `_coalesce.wgsl`, including
      the ORPHAN pass -- whose whole reason for existing is a convex edge,
      and a level-2 region inside a level-1 tile has convex edges that the
      dense-parent case never saw at a tile boundary.
      GATE: `analyze-d3-interface.js` at the L1/L2 seam -- mass AND momentum
      drift at the f32 floor, and the field error tracking a no-interface
      control. Not Cd, and not a conservation check alone: M4.1b was exactly
      conservative while carrying an O(1) seam defect, and the geometry
      ladder could not tell the two apart.

- [x] **M5.3 — the recursive `S_Advance` schedule. DONE 2026-09-10.**
      Replaced the flattened depth-2 and depth-3 stanzas in `encodeSteps`
      with a level walk: explode -> recurse into m+1 twice -> coalesce ->
      step(m). There is no recursion in
      WebGPU command encoding, so this is a real generalization rather than
      another hardcoded stanza -- 2D's M7, and its warning applies unchanged:
      this is the highest-risk stage for silent ordering bugs, and this
      project has been bitten by a same-dispatch race twice now
      (`amr_manage.wgsl`'s coarsen/refine split, and M4.2b-ii's fill/clear).
      GATE MET: `validate-3d.js` all 34 cases print every figure
      BIT-IDENTICALLY, `?levels=2` and `?levels=3` alike -- the depth-3
      conservation rows reproduce M5.2b's hand-driven numbers exactly
      (2.003e-1 / 2.938e-4 and 1.704e-1 / 1.949e-4).

      **THE RECURSION IS ON THE HOST, and that dissolves the difficulty the
      2D plan expected.** Its M7 says "there is no recursion in WebGPU
      command encoding, so depth 3+ needs a real generalization". True, and
      it does not follow that the SCHEDULE cannot recurse: the host emits a
      FLAT command stream, so a recursive emitter is exactly the right
      shape. `advanceLevel(m, parity)` is eleven lines -- explode(m+1),
      recurse twice, coalesce(m+1), step(m) -- and level 0 is the base case
      because L0 genuinely is different (dense, no ring, no tiles; the 2D
      arc's decision 1).

      **DEPTH 4 RUNS AND WAS NEVER WRITTEN.** `?levels=4` was explicitly
      refused before this; now it boots, allocates L1 64 / L2 512 / L3 512
      tiles on a 16^3 beltrami box, and steps finite. That is the return on
      the stage: the flattened stanza was already 14 dispatches in a fixed
      order at depth 3, and depth 4 would have been another copy.

      THE BLANKET REFUSAL IS LIFTED and replaced by two NAMED ones, so every
      depth a run can reach is a depth something validates: `?levels>=3`
      with a BODY needs per-level force with finest-wins masking (M5.4), and
      with `?dynamic=1` needs the balance pass (M5.5). The second is
      UNREACHABLE today -- `?dynamic=1` already requires `?refine=body`, so
      the body refusal always fires first -- and is kept anyway, because it
      stops being unreachable exactly when M5.4 lifts the other one.

      **AND 4.2a's CHECKERS ARE NO LONGER VACUOUS.** M4.2b-iv wrote down in
      advance that the gate for the cascade landing is
      `validate-d3-invariants.js` ceasing to print VACUOUS. New `box3` and
      `bar3` configs at `?levels=3` do exactly that: 2:1 balance and ring
      parent coverage both report plain `ok` for the first time, against a
      tree read back from the GPU rather than from the host's copy. Static
      refinement is enough -- the claim being checked is that
      `refineHierarchy`'s tree survives the trip, which is a different claim
      from the host-side unit tests. The pool column now reports every
      level, since one count hides the distribution entirely.

**M5.4 — THE BODY LIVES ENTIRELY ON THE FINEST LEVEL.** A user decision,
2026-09-10, and it REPLACES the per-level force plan below rather than
refining it. The reasoning, in the user's terms: nobody has the basis to
decide what near-body criterion should govern refining or coarsening a
block that contains the body, nor how a coarse/fine interface should behave
with a body crossing it. So neither question is answered -- both are made
unreachable.

WHAT MAKES THIS CHEAP IS THAT THE CONSTRAINT ALREADY EXISTS. M5.1b evaluates
the geometry criterion at the FINEST level only and derives every coarser
level from `cascade21`; coarser levels are the 2:1 and ring buffer and
nothing else. So the body's shell is already entirely on the finest level BY
CONSTRUCTION, already checked at cell granularity against the SDF
(`checkGeometryCoverage`), already maintained under motion by the
refine-ahead term (M4.2b-iii), and already asserted at depth 3 by
`make test`. M5.4 stops treating that as a happy accident and starts
depending on it.

TWO CONSEQUENCES, and the second is the one that pays:
- **ONE force pass, at the finest level.** No finest-wins masking anywhere:
  `forceCoveredByFiner` goes, and so do the N-1 per-level force pipelines
  the old plan would have added. The dx^(D-1) weight becomes one override
  instead of a per-level table.
- **NO COARSE/FINE SEAM EVER TOUCHES THE BODY OR ITS MARGIN.** The refined
  region is the body dilated by `margin`, so the seam is in pure fluid by
  construction. explode/coalesce never has to reason about solid cells at an
  interface -- a question this milestone would otherwise have had to answer.

THE PRICE, stated plainly: the failure mode gets worse. Before, a shell that
failed to cover part of the body degraded force ACCURACY, because the coarse
pass still integrated the uncovered part. Now it degrades to force MISSING.
That is why M5.4a is a precondition and not a nicety.

- [x] **M5.4a — running out of pool slots is a HARD FAILURE. DONE
      2026-09-10.** Wanted independently of the body decision: the user's 2D
      experience is that an exhausted pool does not drift, it "becomes very
      quickly wildly inaccurate and diverging".

      M4.2b-i deliberately made the out-of-slots path degrade to "less
      refined than asked for" rather than to a scrambled free list. The free
      list is still protected -- the counter is restored and the block stays
      coarse -- but the event is now RECORDED (`freeCount[1]`, a sticky
      refusal count) and the host stops on it: `#status` gets a specific
      `error:` naming the refusal count, the step, and `?slotHeadroom=`;
      `live` stops; `debugStepSync` stops advancing and returns
      `{step, exhausted}`; `debugPoolState` reports `slotsExhausted`.

      RECORDED RATHER THAN INFERRED from `debugCheckGeometryCoverage`
      failing, which is a different claim: coverage says the constraint is
      violated NOW, this says the allocator is the reason.

      STOPS ADVANCING RATHER THAN THROWING. A tool reading the step count or
      the pool state sees it immediately; one doing neither gets a run that
      visibly goes nowhere with `error:` in the status. Throwing would make
      the config that EXPECTS exhaustion harder to express than the failure
      it tests.

      GATE MET, and it is the old config turned inside out:
      `validate-d3-invariants.js`'s `body-refine` asks for far more tiles
      than the headroom allows. It used to assert that the pool stayed
      consistent while quietly under-refining; it now asserts the refusal
      FIRES (`expectExhausted`), while still asserting pool consistency. All
      9 configs PASS, `body-refine` reporting `EXHAUSTED(ok)`. The AMR and
      sphere cases are unmoved.

      `assertPageHealthy` grew an `allowStatus` regexp for this: a config
      whose point is to trip a guard is otherwise indistinguishable from one
      that broke. It suppresses only the status line -- uncaught exceptions
      and console errors still fail, because nothing legitimately expects
      those.

- [x] **M5.4b — one force pass, at the finest level. DONE 2026-09-10, and
      the -1.4% it left red is EXPLAINED rather than tolerated.** A body at `?levels=3` runs, is
      imposed correctly (verified by screenshot: sphere, dipole, wake) and
      integrates a force. It does not yet reproduce the dense reference's Cd
      to the 0.5% the depth-2 case holds to.

      WHAT LANDED: exactly one force pass, at the finest level, whenever
      there is a pool. `forceCoveredByFiner` and its five overrides and its
      `blockSlot` binding are gone from the coarse kernel, which under AMR is
      no longer dispatched at all. `DX_WEIGHT` became an override (4^-m, not
      L1's baked 0.25 -- at depth 3 that would have reported FOUR TIMES the
      drag). The two preconditions are enforced: a body with AMR is refused
      without `?refine=body`, and a margin below the force stencil's reach is
      refused.

      **THE DEPTH-2 RESULT IS THE EVIDENCE THE REQUIREMENT WAS ALREADY
      HOLDING:** `sphere-amr-Re20-D16` is BIT-IDENTICAL at Cd 2.9416 with the
      coarse pass deleted, which says it had been contributing exactly zero
      -- the body was already entirely inside L1.

      **A ZERO THAT CAME FROM TWO PLACES AT ONCE, and it cost a cycle.** The
      first depth-3 measurement was Cd EXACTLY 0.0000.
      `fineToCoarseUnit3` returns a position in the PARENT LEVEL's units, and
      the body lives in L0 units; at level 1 those coincide, below it they do
      not, and the map is AFFINE (L0 = 0.5u - 0.25 per rung) because
      refinement is cell-centred. That was wrong in THREE places -- the step
      kernel's own position, the step kernel's neighbour position, and the
      force kernel's -- and fixing the first two left the answer at exactly
      0.0000, because the force kernel's NEIGHBOUR position (the bounce-back
      link test, "does this cell have a solid neighbour") was still in the
      wrong frame, so no cell ever had one. Identical symptom before and
      after real progress. `L0_SCALE`/`L0_OFFSET` are now overrides on both
      kernels, identity at level 1, which is why every depth-2 number is
      untouched.

      **WHAT IS RULED OUT, measured:**
      - NOT the seam. Doubling the margin (a 45% wider shell at depth 3)
        moves the force by 0.1%, at BOTH depths:

            levels  margin  finest tiles       fx      rel
              2       2          160       0.739330  1.00000
              2       4          232       0.738398  0.99874
              3       2         1280       0.729247  0.98636
              3       4         1856       0.728457  0.98529

      - NOT the weight. A wrong `DX_WEIGHT` is a factor of 4, not 1.4%.
      - NOT the frame map, which was re-derived independently against
        d3-amr.mjs's own convention: level-2 global index g sits at L0
        0.25g - 0.375, and the composed transform gives exactly that.

      **THE THING TO EXPLAIN, and it is a real oddity rather than a
      tolerance question.** Surface resolution across the diameter is 16
      cells dense, 32 at `?levels=2`, 64 at `?levels=3`. The dense series
      FALLS with resolution (D16 2.9436 -> D24 2.9067). But `?levels=2`,
      at 32-across, sits at 2.9416 -- indistinguishable from dense D16 --
      while `?levels=3` at 64-across drops to 2.9015, close to dense D24's
      value. So refining the shell once changes Cd by 0.07% and refining it
      twice changes it by 1.4%. Those two facts cannot both be "resolution",
      and until one of them is explained the tolerance must not be widened.

      **THE EXPERIMENT, AND IT ANSWERED CLEANLY: THE FLOW DIFFERS.** Velocity
      fields at 4000 steps, relative L2 of the difference bucketed by
      distance from the surface, at stride 4:

          bucket        inside   0-1    1-2    2-4    4-8   8-16    >16
          L2 vs dense   6.5e-1 1.3e-1 4.3e-2 1.8e-2 8.3e-3 1.8e-3 1.4e-4
          L3 vs dense   6.5e-1 3.9e-1 6.7e-2 2.6e-2 1.2e-2 4.8e-3 6.8e-4
          L3 vs L2      6.0e-7 4.8e-1 2.3e-2 9.0e-3 7.8e-3 3.9e-3 6.0e-4

      Refining the shell changes the near-wall solution by 48% one cell off
      the surface, decaying to 6e-4 far away and to 6e-7 INSIDE the body
      (where both hold the same solid). So the force integral is reporting a
      real change, not mis-integrating one.

      **AND IT CONVERGES, at first order.** Run at D=8 where depth 4 fits:

          levels  finest dx      fx        vs prev
            1       1.000    0.189120
            2       0.500    0.191727     +1.378%
            3       0.250    0.186495     -2.729%
            4       0.125    0.184122     -1.272%

      The increments HALVE after the coarsest step, which is exactly what a
      staircased bounce-back sphere does. **The levels 1 -> 2 step is simply
      not on the asymptotic trend, at either diameter** -- which is the whole
      explanation of the oddity above. Depth 2 reproducing the dense Cd to
      0.07% is an accident of that first refinement being near-neutral, not
      a law, and the 0.5% bound had quietly encoded it as one.

      SO THE DEPTH-3 BOUND IS 3%, AND THAT IS A DIFFERENT CLAIM RATHER THAN
      A WIDENED TOLERANCE. What the case exists to catch is a wrong
      cross-level WEIGHT, which is a factor of 2 or 4 (`cd_with_dx3_weight`
      is exactly half); 3% catches that with 17x to spare while admitting
      the 1.4% the physics actually moves. The evidence is recorded in
      `benchmarks/d3.json`'s `sphere_amr_note` so the bound can be argued
      with rather than inherited.

      ALL 35 CASES PASS.

**Superseded by M5.4, kept as the record of what was planned:**

- [ ] **~~force integration at depth, finest-wins masking~~.** Delete
      `forceCoveredByFiner` and the coarse force dispatch whenever the body
      is geometry-refined; bind the pool force pass to the FINEST level and
      make `DX_WEIGHT` an override (4^-m, not L1's baked 0.25). Refuse a
      body with AMR unless `?refine=body`, and refuse a margin below the
      force stencil's reach -- the two preconditions that turn the invariant
      from an assumption into an enforced one.
