      GATE: sphere Cd at `?levels=3` against the dense reference, plus the
      partition-invariance check (the same configuration must integrate to
      the same force however the hierarchy is cut around it).

**Superseded by M5.4, kept as the record of what was planned:**

- [ ] **~~force integration at depth, finest-wins masking~~.** M4.1d
      already put the body force on the fine level with weight dx^(D-1);
      what is missing at depth is the masking. `average`/`coalesce` keep a
      parent's cells populated under an active child, so running every
      level's force pass unconditionally sums the same drag once crudely at
      the parent and once accurately at the child. Each level skips any cell
      with an active child one level down -- the same "which level owns this
      point" rule the render compositing needs, built once.
      GATE: sphere Cd at `?levels=3` against the dense reference, at a
      deliberately coarse L0 -- the aliasing regime this exists to fix -- and
      the invariance check 2D's M8 names: the same physical configuration
      must integrate to the same force however the hierarchy happens to be
      partitioned around it.

- [x] **M5.5a — the 2:1 cascade as a KERNEL, scored against the host. DONE
      2026-09-10.** `completeOctets` and `balance` in
      `shaders/common_d3_manage.wgsl`, the GPU mirror of `cascade21`.

      **BOTH PASSES ARE GATHERS, one thread per OUTPUT block**, reading
      inputs they do not write -- so there is no scatter and no race to
      reason about, which is the opposite of how the 2D manager does it.
      Inverting cascade21's scatter (present(m,b) => present(m-1,
      parent(b+d)) for all 27 offsets d) gives the gather: parent p is
      wanted if ANY of the 64 child blocks in [2p-1, 2p+2]^3 is. **64 and
      not 8**, because d = 0 is the tree property, the six faces are 2:1
      balance, and the twelve edges and eight corners are the RING (M5.2a).

      ONLY THE FINEST LEVEL EVALUATES THE CRITERION (M5.1b), so `decide` is
      one pipeline and every coarser level is derived. That is what makes
      this small.

      GATE MET, and it is the strongest shape available: `debugRunBalance`
      dispatches the chain and reads every level's want set back, and
      `validate-d3-invariants.js` scores it against `refineHierarchy` --
      two independently-written statements of one rule, run against each
      other on real GPU data. EXACT agreement, zero blocks differing in
      either direction, at depths 2, 3 and 4, on a pinned sphere and on the
      drifting body:

          sphere L2     L1 160/160
          sphere L3     L1 432/432   L2 1280/1280
          sphere L4     L1 312/312   L2  704/704    L3 1856/1856
          drift  L3     L1 432/432   L2 1280/1280

      New `body3` config: the depth-3 row where all four checks are real at
      once -- 2:1 balance, ring parents, cell-granular geometry coverage at
      the FINEST level (M5.4's hard requirement), and the closure.

      TWO THINGS WORTH CARRYING:
      - **The criterion's block box uses a PURE SCALE, not M5.4b's affine
        map, and that is deliberate.** The step and force kernels convert a
        cell CENTRE and must land on it exactly; this converts a
        conservative BOUNDING BOX whose only hard requirement is that it
        AGREE with `refineHierarchy`, which uses the same pure scale. The
        difference is the cell-centring offset, under half an L0 cell at
        depth 3 against a margin of 2, and the real guarantee is the
        cell-granular coverage check either way.
      - **A bind group that aliases one buffer as both writable and
        read-only makes WebGPU SKIP the dispatch.** Binding 5 is this
        level's want and binding 7 the child's; at the deepest level there
        is no child, and binding the level to itself produced a manager
        that decided NOTHING, silently, at every depth including 2. A
        4-byte dummy sits there now.

- [x] **M5.5b — the allocator at depth. DONE 2026-09-10, and it found a
      live defect in the depth-2 drain it was extending.**

      **THE ONLY NEW KERNEL IS A POOL-PARENT `average`**, and it is new the
      way every other pool-parent kernel here is new: `common_d3_amr_average`
      stopped naming `coarseCell` and `mac_coarse` and now calls
      `parentIndex` / `parentMacStore`, so
      `d3_amr_average_pool_q{19,27}.wgsl` is the same body assembled with
      `common_d3_parent_pool.wgsl` + `common_d3_parentmac_pool.wgsl`. The
      FILL needed nothing at all -- it is M5.2b-ii's seed pipeline with
      `NEW_ONLY`, over the same bind group, because a tile being born wants
      exactly the transfer that seeded the level.

      Binding 4 is now the parent's `mac` in every kernel that writes one,
      average as well as coalesce. That is not tidiness: it is the only way
      the `array<vec4<f32>>` / `array<f32>` type split can do its job, and
      that split is the whole defence against the transposition recorded
      above.

      **TWO SWEEP ORDERS, AND THEY ARE OPPOSITE.** Drain+coarsen run
      FINEST-FIRST, because a dying tile restricts into its PARENT and the
      parent must still hold its slot when it does -- a whole subtree can
      die in one event, and finest-first collapses it a rung at a time (L3
      into L2, then L2, now carrying what L3 handed it, into L1).
      Refine+fill run COARSEST-FIRST, the mirror: a new tile is interpolated
      FROM its parent, and a newly-refined L2 tile can be the parent of a
      newly-refined L3 tile in the same event. Neither order is a
      preference; each is the only one that does not read a slot that has
      been handed to someone else.

      **THE CLEAR IS A KERNEL, NOT A `writeBuffer`** (`clearWant`).
      `balance` ORs into its level's want, so every derived level must start
      at zero -- and `device.queue.writeBuffer` is ordered at SUBMIT, not
      where it is called, while the manager is encoded in the middle of a
      command buffer holding hundreds of macro-steps. A host-side clear
      would have zeroed whichever step was encoded last and left every other
      step's cascade accumulating into stale data. `debugRunBalance` got
      away with one because it submits alone; it now shares
      `encodeDecideChain` with the solver, so the chain it scores is the
      chain that runs.

      **THE DEFECT: THE DEPTH-2 DRAIN WAS DISPATCHED OVER THE COARSE GRID.**
      M4.2b-ii encoded it through `gridPass`, which dispatches `[NX/4, NY/4,
      NZ/4]`, and `average`'s own shape is `(per, per, per * slots)` with
      `per = ceil(RB/4) = 1`. So the slot index was `wgid.z` and the pass
      covered the first `NZ/4` slots and silently skipped every one above:
      4 of 160 on `body-coarsen`, 6 of 240 on `drift`. A tile coarsened out
      of a skipped slot was freed without its solution ever reaching L0.
      **Nothing in the suite could see it** -- those configs are structural,
      and the pool stays perfectly consistent while the FIELD quietly loses
      a tile's worth of fluid; `sphere-amr-dynamic` is bit-identical to the
      static run because its body is PINNED and nothing ever drains. The
      same "a count is not a set" lesson, one level down: a dispatch that is
      the right SHAPE for one buffer is not thereby the right shape for
      another.

      **AND `reset()` RESTORED `freeCount` WITHOUT RESTORING THE POOL.** It
      re-zeroed level 1's free counter while leaving `blockSlot` /
      `slotToBlock` wherever the manager had left them, which is a free list
      whose entries are slots still in use. `uploadLevelTopology` now puts
      every level back, and it runs BEFORE the seed rather than after --
      otherwise the seed fills the tiles the manager happened to leave and
      the upload then relabels them. Static runs never noticed either
      problem, because nothing there ever changed.

      Also per level now, and each for its own reason: the coarsen/refine/
      clear pipelines (they read `nbx()` from `NX`, so a level-2 dispatch
      through a level-1 pipeline walks an eighth of the blocks and calls the
      rest unrefined) and the slot-exhaustion check (each level owns a
      refusal counter, and the level that runs out is the FINEST one -- the
      one the criterion drives directly).

      GATE MET. New `drift3`: the drift sphere at `?levels=3&dynamic=1`,
      where the finest level's shell must FOLLOW the body and every check is
      real at once. 2:1 balance ok, ring parents ok, cell-granular geometry
      coverage ok (908 cells required), pool consistent throughout, field
      finite, and the finest level's bounding box moved `lo.x 2 -> 8` -- six
      L2 blocks, which is the 12 L0 cells the body travelled. `want vs host`
      matched exactly at both levels (L1 208/208, L2 448/448) on a tree the
      MANAGER rebuilt every 4 steps rather than one the host uploaded. Its
      control `body3-dynamic` -- the same depth-3 tree, pinned body, manager
      running -- decides "no change" at every level and stays green, which
      is the one answer that can be checked against a static run.

      The depth-2 suite is unchanged: all six structural configs PASS, and
      `sphere-amr-dynamic-Re20-D16` is still BIT-IDENTICAL to
      `sphere-amr-Re20-D16` (M4.2b-i's gate) through the binding renumber
      and the dispatch fix. `amr-box-RB4`, which is pinned to
      `?interface=interp` and therefore the only case that still runs the
      dense `average` in anger, passes unchanged.

      **The bbox expectation now reads the FINEST level**, not level 1. The
      criterion is evaluated there and every coarser level is derived from
      it, so that is where "the shell followed the body" is a statement
      about the manager rather than about the closure. Identical at
      `?levels=2`.

**Superseded by M5.5a's kernel, kept as the shape it was planned in:**

- [ ] **~~the manager at depth, and VACUOUS goes away~~.** Mirror
      `cascade21` into `common_d3_manage.wgsl` as the `balance` pass, between
      `decide` and `drain`, where M4.2b-iv's header already says it goes.
      Octet-granular allocation.
      GATE: `validate-d3-invariants.js` stops printing VACUOUS and the 2:1
      check is a real gate for the first time. If it lands and that line does
      NOT change, either the manager is not building multi-level trees or the
      checker is not seeing what it writes -- M4.2b-iv wrote that sentence
      down in advance precisely so it cannot be explained away here.

- [x] **M5.6 — validation and tooling at depth. DONE 2026-09-10, and TWO OF
      ITS THREE CLAUSES TURNED OUT TO DESCRIBE THINGS THAT DO NOT EXIST.**
      Written in advance of M5, and reality moved; what follows is what is
      actually there, and what the other two clauses should have said.

      **`validate-3d.js` AT DEPTH 3 -- two gaps, both filled.** The depth-3
      rows that existed (`sphere-amr-L3`, `amr-bar/box-explode-L3`) covered
      Cd and CONSERVATION; nothing covered the FIELD at depth, and nothing
      covered the depth-3 MANAGER against a field at all.

      - `amr-box-explode-field-L3` (N=32). The depth-2 field case's claim one
        rung deeper, and at depth the box case has TWO seams rather than one:
        the criterion is evaluated at the finest level and every coarser
        level is whatever `cascade21` requires, so L1's refined region is
        strictly larger than L2's footprint. Worst-checkpoint field L2rel
        **6.137e-3 against its own no-interface control (`refine=all`,
        levels=3) at 5.590e-3 -- +9.8%**, inside the same 8-10% band the
        one-seam depth-2 case holds to. The depth-2 run of the SAME case and
        resolution is 4.544e-3, so the depth-3 number is not a regression
        against depth 2 either: it is a different discretization with a
        genuinely finer inner region and one more seam. The decay rate is
        where the seam shows -- 4.832e-3 against the control's -9.501e-4,
        which flips sign. REPEAT-CHECKED bit-identical, so the bounds are
        tight on purpose.
      - `sphere-amr-dynamic-L3-Re20-D16`. The M4.2b-i bit-identical gate
        against a manager that now runs at EVERY level: decide-and-close over
        the tree, then drain/coarsen finest-first and refine/fill
        coarsest-first. Far more machinery to perturb the answer than at
        depth 2, and none of it may. **Cd 2.9015, bit-identical to the static
        depth-3 run.** It is the PHYSICS partner to `body3-dynamic` and
        `drift3`, neither of which scores a field -- a manager that moves
        tiles correctly but TRANSFERS them wrongly is invisible to both.

      **`readPoolStats` WAS READING ONE LEVEL AND THE WRONG SLOTS.** It
      walked level 1's `mac` over `[0, activeSlots)`, which is wrong twice
      over: a blowup born on level 2 was invisible to it, and under
      `?dynamic=1` a slot below `activeSlots` may be FREE (holding a dead
      tile's leftovers) while one above it is in use, so the statistics were
      taken over the wrong set of cells entirely. It now reads every level
      over the SLOT BUDGET, honouring `slotToBlock`, and
      `validate-d3-invariants.js` calls it at every checkpoint -- printing
      each level's rms and failing on a non-finite or non-advancing one. It
      had NO caller before, which is why none of that had ever mattered;
      "tooling at depth" has to include giving the tooling something to do.

      A pool's `mac` is DERIVED -- written by the step and coalesce kernels,
      not by `reset()`, which seeds `f` only -- so every level reads rms 0 at
      step 0 even on a case whose L0 energy is nonzero, and is correct by the
      first substep. The not-advancing check skips step 0 for that reason and
      says so, rather than being quietly loosened.

      `debugPoolState`'s per-level `byLevel` already existed (M5.1c); what
      M5.6 did was move the TOOL onto it. `expectInUse` and `expectBboxMove`
      now read the FINEST level, because that is where the criterion is
      evaluated and therefore the only level where "the manager acted" is a
      statement about the criterion rather than about the closure propagating
      something that happened below. Identical at `?levels=2`.

      **CLAUSE 2 WAS WRONG: THERE IS NO 3D SNAPSHOT TO VERSION.**
      `debugSnapshotLoad`/`amr-snapshot.js` are 2D only, and they exist
      because `validate-divergence.js` and `validate-amr-vs-dense.js` need to
      seed two solvers from one state. 3D has neither tool. Nothing was
      built, deliberately: a serialization format with no reader is a
      liability, not a milestone. **The advice in the clause is still
      right and is re-homed rather than dropped** -- whoever writes the first
      3D snapshot should index by level and make the format version REJECT an
      old snapshot rather than misread it, because a two-level snapshot
      loaded into a three-level run is exactly the silent-nonsense shape M5.0
      refused `?levels=3` to avoid.

      **CLAUSE 3 WAS WRONG: THE SLICE RENDERER COMPOSITES ZERO TIERS, NOT
      TWO, AND IT DOES NOT NEED TO.** `shaders/d3_render_slice.wgsl` binds
      the dense `mac` and nothing else, and so does every host readback
      (`readSubsampled`, `readDuctProfile`, `readStats`). It does not need a
      tier walk because **the coalesce chain already carries every level's
      solution up to L0**: level m's coalesce republishes the macroscopic
      field into its PARENT's `mac` under the refined region, so L2 reaches
      L1 reaches L0. That is not read off the code alone -- it is what
      `amr-box-explode-field-L3` measures, since with `refine=all` at
      levels=3 the dense `mac` is written ENTIRELY by two coalesce hops and
      still scores 5.59e-3 against the analytic solution.

      So there is no two-tier compositing to generalize to N. The real and
      different limitation is that a refined region is DISPLAYED at L0
      resolution -- correct values, coarse pixels. Fixing that means
      resolving a sample point down the tree in the fragment shader, which is
      **M6's sampler** (the GPU sibling of `field-reconstruct.js`) in two
      dimensions instead of three. Building a throwaway version of it here
      was declined; M6 should do it once.

**M6 — Resample-and-raymarch volume rendering.** GPU sibling of
`tools/lib/field-reconstruct.js`. Staged, because the sampler underneath it
is the part with a checkable answer and the raymarcher is the part with a
picture.

- [x] **M6.0a — a pool level's `mac` is SEEDED, not left derived. DONE
      2026-09-10.** `shaders/common_d3_moments.wgsl`, run by `reset()` after
      the interp seed has cascaded down the levels.

      `mac` is written by the step kernel for the cells a level solves and by
      the child's coalesce for the cells the child covers, so it is right
      from the first substep and simply UNWRITTEN before then. `reset()`
      seeded `f` and stopped. That was invisible while nothing read a pool's
      `mac` off the GPU and stops being invisible the moment something does:
      `readPoolStats` reported rms 0 on every level at step 0 (M5.6 had to
      special-case it), and the sampler below would have rendered a refined
      region as a HOLE at step 0 -- black where the coarse field around it is
      not, which looks exactly like a broken sampler.

      Neither is a reason to loosen a check or special-case a viewer.
      `validate-d3-invariants.js`'s not-advancing check now runs FROM STEP 0,
      with `startsAtRest` naming the two configs where zero is the truth.
      Measured: `body3` step 0 now reads rms 5.00e-2 on L1 and L2, which is
      u0 exactly.

- [x] **M6.0b — the tree sampler, and the slice view on it. DONE
      2026-09-10.** `shaders/common_d3_tree_sample.wgsl`: given a continuous
      point, the finest level with a tile covering it, and that level's
      value and cell size.

      **THE ONLY PLACE IN THIS CODEBASE THAT SPANS LEVELS.** Every solver
      kernel is written for one level and reaches its parent through
      `common_d3_parent_{dense,pool}.wgsl`, which is what makes them all the
      same code at every depth. A viewer cannot do that: "the best answer
      available here" is a question about the whole tree at once.

      **THE FRAME IS ONE LINE, AND IT IS FORCED BY `fineToCoarseUnit`.** That
      map puts the two children of parent cell `origin` at origin -+ 1/4, so
      cell centres are at INTEGERS and a cell spans [c - 1/2, c + 1/2).
      Hence `centre(g, m) = (g + 1/2) * 2^-m - 1/2`, and inverting,
      `g(p, m) = floor((p + 1/2) * 2^m)`. Ownership is then ONE DIVISION by
      `2*RB` -- the GLOBAL FINE route -- which is what makes it impossible
      for a viewer to land in a tile's RING, where the value is filled
      rather than solved. Not a bounds test; a consequence of the frame.

      **GATED TWO WAYS, AND THE LEVEL IS THE CLAIM.** `d3-amr.mjs` gained
      `cellAtLevel` and `finestLevelAt` -- the host statement of the same
      rule. `make test` scores `cellAtLevel` against the PER-RUNG AFFINE MAP
      (`L0 = 0.5u - 0.25`, the one `common_d3_force_pool.wgsl` uses and whose
      absence left the integrated force at exactly zero), checks the local
      index is always interior, checks the periodic wrap is against the
      LEVEL's dims rather than L0's, and MUTATION-CHECKS three plausible
      slips -- corners instead of centres, round instead of floor, and the
      parent frame's scale. Then `debugCheckTreeSample` runs the real shader
      over 1728 points through a probe compute pass
      (`common_d3_tree_probe.wgsl`, the same function over the same
      bindings) and scores it against `finestLevelAt` on a blockSlot
      readback. EXACT agreement on every config, at depth 2 and 3, static and
      manager-rebuilt:

          box    1512 / 216
          body   1720 / 8
          body3  1681 / 30 / 17
          drift3  896 / 608 / 224

      The histogram is not decoration. A sampler that fell back to L0
      everywhere would AGREE with a host handed the same level sets, so the
      tool fails a pooled run whose points never left level 0 -- the
      "`?refine=all` cannot see an interface bug" lesson in another costume.

      **THE VORTICITY STEP IS THE LOCAL CELL SIZE**, returned by the sampler
      alongside the value. A fixed one-L0-cell step throws away exactly the
      resolution this change adds; a fixed finest-cell step samples the same
      coarse cell twice outside the refined region and paints the whole
      coarse field flat zero. Neither is a small error.

      **ELEVEN STORAGE BUFFERS IN A COMPUTE STAGE**, past the WebGPU spec
      minimum of 8. `main-3d.js` now requests 12 and fails loud, the
      treatment `main-amr.js` already settled on (it asks for 16 and runs on
      the phone). Worth knowing because a bind-group-layout failure is NOT an
      exception -- it poisons every pipeline built on it, and the only symptom
      is `Invalid PipelineLayout is invalid due to a previous error`.

      MEASURED, not just gated: at `?levels=3` the sphere renders with a
      crisp surface and a thin near-wall vorticity sheet at level-2
      resolution, against the dense run's staircase of L0 blocks, with the
      refined shell's seam faintly visible around it -- which is the view
      doing its job. Physics is untouched: `amr-all-RB4`, `amr-box-RB4`,
      both `amr-*-explode` conservation cases and both field cases return
      bit-identical numbers.

      **AND THE SEAM WAS NOT "FAINTLY VISIBLE", IT WAS WRONG -- TWICE, IN
      THE SAME EXPRESSION** (fixed 2026-09-11). Both halves come from one
      fact: **sampleTree is NEAREST, so it answers at a CELL CENTRE, not at
      the query, and a cell centre differs from the query on ALL THREE
      AXES.** A caller taking a finite difference does not know its own
      stencil -- it knows what it asked for, not what it got.

      1. **THE DIVISOR.** The difference took its step from the CENTRE
         sample's level and divided by `2h` regardless of where the arms
         landed. Across a seam the `+h` arm can land in a coarser cell, so
         the true separation is 0.5 to 1.5 times `2h`. `TreeSample` now
         carries `c`, the centre of the cell the value came from, in the
         query's own continuous L0 frame and UNWRAPPED, and the renderer
         differences over the true separation.

      2. **THE OTHER TWO AXES, which the divisor cannot reach because it is
         in the NUMERATOR.** A coarse cell's centre and a fine cell's centre
         also differ ALONG THE SLICE NORMAL, by up to a quarter of a coarse
         cell. So a pair straddling a seam computes

             est(du/db) = du/db + (da/db_step) du/da + (dn/db_step) du/dn

         with `db_step` around one -- an O(1) contamination of an in-plane
         derivative by the TRANSVERSE gradients, and the slice normal is the
         one direction the view never shows. The fix is to RE-ASK, not to
         correct: `sampleTreeAtMost(p, mMax)` caps the walk, and `dPlane`
         re-asks both arms at the coarser of the two levels when they
         disagree. Two samples at the same level share the transverse offset
         exactly, so it cancels, and the in-plane separation is then exactly
         `2h` by construction. One re-ask per seam pixel; none anywhere else.

      **SCORED AGAINST A FIELD WITH EXACT VORTICITY, not against another
      discretization.** Beltrami is `curl(u) = k*u`, so `omega_z = k*w` is
      known in closed form at every point. `n=48 ?levels=2 ?refine=box`,
      step 200, error as a percentage of `max|omega|`, over the 572 seam
      pixels (6% of the slice) and over the rest:

          stencil                        seam rms   seam max   elsewhere
          divide by 2h (before)            11.26%     25.63%       0.30%
          true separation (fix 1 alone)     5.37%     10.23%       0.30%
          level-consistent pair (both)      0.73%      2.69%       0.30%

      and the residual 2.69% is at the non-seam maximum (1.96%), i.e. the
      floor. Two stencils tried and rejected on the way are worth recording
      because they look right and are not: widening the step to the coarsest
      level involved without capping the LEVEL reached 7.91% (the arms can
      still come back at different levels), and a non-uniform 3-point
      formula through the centre sample reached 10.43% (it fixes the
      evaluation POINT, which was never the error).

      **THE PREDICTION IS WHAT MAKES IT A DIAGNOSIS.** At one seam pixel the
      contaminating term is computable in closed form -- `ux` carries
      `sin(k z)`, the coarse arm sits at `z = 24.0` and the fine arm at
      `z = 24.25`, and the injected slope is that difference over the
      1.25-cell separation. Predicted `+10.00%` of `max|omega|`; measured
      `+9.94%`. Nothing else was left to find.

      **AND IT IS PROVABLY LOCAL.** A before/after screenshot difference of
      the same paused state is EXACTLY a one-to-two-pixel outline of the
      refined box and black everywhere else: on a dense run, inside a
      uniformly refined region, and anywhere the two arms agree in level,
      the new path is the old arithmetic. No validated number can move.

      **THE TELL WAS A FIELD THAT CANNOT EXPRESS THE ARTIFACT.** `speed` is
      a point sample with no derivative in it, and it is smooth across the
      very seams where `vorticity` is not -- which separates a rendering bug
      from a solver one before any code is read. The solver's own interface
      error was measured alongside and is NOT the cause: sampled `|u - u_ex|`
      is 0.72% of amplitude in the interior against 1.20% in the seam layer,
      a factor of 1.7, nowhere near the 25% the picture showed.

      **STILL NO GATE.** Every number above came from a host reproduction of
      the fragment shader's arithmetic driven through `debugSampleTree`, not
      from a check that runs itself. `debugCheckTreeSample` covers the
      SAMPLER (and still returns `differ: 0`, which is what says the
      `sampleTree` -> `sampleTreeAtMost` refactor is a no-op); nothing covers
      the DIFFERENCE built on it. M6.1-M6.3 need the same rule -- any finite
      difference off this sampler must come from a level-consistent pair --
      so the gate belongs with them rather than here.

      **AND THE PICTURE NOW TURNS SO THAT DOWN IS DOWN** (2026-09-11).
      `?rotate=0|90|180|270` degrees clockwise, defaulted per scenario from a
      new `down` field: `fall` declares `[1, 0, 0]` and the z-slice turns a
      quarter, so a body falling along +x falls down the WINDOW instead of
      tracking across it.

      **IT IS AN IMAGE ROTATION AND THAT IS THE ENTIRE SAFETY ARGUMENT.**
      Every field this view draws -- |u|, the OUT-OF-PLANE vorticity, the
      velocity along the slice NORMAL -- is invariant under a rotation inside
      the slice plane, so the change is confined to `uvToPlane` and not one
      line of the sampling or the differencing moves. `rot` is the rotation
      subgroup on purpose: a REFLECTION would flip the sign of omega and the
      colour ramp would then lie, which is exactly the class of bug this view
      exists to catch in the solver.

      Two things that are easy to get wrong here and were:
      - **THE CANVAS ASPECT MUST FOLLOW THE TURN.** `planeExtent` swaps its
        two extents for an odd turn. Without that the canvas keeps the
        unrotated ratio and the picture STRETCHES rather than rotating -- a
        sphere as an ellipse, which is the same distortion that function's
        own history is about.
      - **THE FOUR CASES ARE NOT GUESSABLE.** `rot = 0` already draws +b
        DOWNWARD (`1 - uv.y`), so the quarter turn hands that role to +a and
        sends +b LEFT; writing the closed forms from "rotate 90 clockwise"
        without checking gets two of the four backwards. Verified on all three
        slice normals: at `?axis=y` the fall axis is already the second
        in-plane axis, so `downTurn` correctly asks for NO turn, and at
        `?axis=x` the fall axis is the slice normal and no turn can help.

      The scenario field is read by the VIEW alone -- the solver has no
      opinion about which way is down and must not acquire one.

#### THE BALLPARK, MEASURED 2026-09-10 BEFORE BUILDING ANY OF IT

Three things that sec 1.3's sketch got wrong or could not have known, and
they set the shape of M6.1-M6.3.

**`r16float` IS NOT STORAGE-WRITABLE IN CORE WEBGPU.** Measured on an
RTX 4080 with a device requesting no optional features: creating a 3D
`r16float` texture with `STORAGE_BINDING` is REJECTED
("incompatible with the format"). It is available under
`texture-formats-tier1`, which that adapter advertises -- but an optional
feature is a dependency, and the mobile target may not have it. What IS core:

    rgba16float   storage-writable AND filterable    8 B/voxel
    r32float      storage-writable, filtering needs
                  the optional float32-filterable    4 B/voxel
    r16float      NOT storage-writable               --

So sec 1.3's "256^3 x 2 B = 32 MB" is really 8 B/voxel for a safe design, 4x
its estimate. `maxTextureDimension3D` is 2048, so DIMENSIONS NEVER BIND here
-- memory and bandwidth do, and the sketch's one worry was the wrong one.

**THE REFINED REGION IS 0.44% OF THE DOMAIN, MEASURED.** `debugPoolState` on
the flagship case (sphere n=16, `?levels=3`, 192x128x128): level 2's
bounding box is 12 blocks per axis at 2 L0 cells each = **24^3 L0 cells**,
against a domain of 192x128x128. Level 1's is 32^3. That single number
decides the whole design:

    full domain, sphere n=16, 12 B/voxel (vel rgba16 + scalar r32)
      1x  (L0)       3.1e6 voxels     36 MB      ~0.8 ms/frame
      2x             2.5e7 voxels    288 MB      ~6.5 ms/frame
      4x  (FINEST)   2.0e8 voxels   2.25 GB       ~52 ms/frame

    the refined shell ALONE, at FULL finest resolution
                     96^3 = 8.8e5     10 MB      ~0.23 ms/frame

**2.25 GB against 10 MB for the same data at the same resolution.** A 225x
gap, and it is exactly the ratio AMR exists to exploit: materializing the
whole domain at the finest level IS the dense run AMR replaced. It was never
the thing to build, and sec 1.3's "~256^3" quietly assumed it.

The ms column is a MODEL, not a measurement -- one tree sample per voxel at
~100 B (the pool `mac` is PLANAR, so one sample is four scattered loads) plus
six coherent neighbour taps. It gets priced by REMOVAL once the pass exists,
which is the only attribution method plans/perf-characterization.md trusts.

**TWO PASSES, NOT ONE.** Q-criterion straight from the tree needs SEVEN tree
samples per voxel (centre plus +-h on three axes). Resampling velocity first
and differencing the dense volume needs ONE tree sample plus six coherent
taps of a texture -- roughly 4x cheaper, for one extra volume. The gradient
is then evaluated once per voxel instead of once per ray sample, which is the
same argument one level up.

One property to state rather than discover: the tree sampler is NEAREST, so
differencing the resampled volume STAIRCASES across a seam. At the default
resolution the refined region is 0.44% of the domain so it is confined, and
it is the same "you can see the seam" property the slice view has -- not a
hidden error.

- [x] **M6.1 — the resample pass. DONE 2026-09-10, and the ballpark held.**
      `shaders/common_d3_resample.wgsl`: the M6.0 sampler over a dense box,
      into an `rgba16float` 3D storage texture. The sampler was already the
      whole of its inner loop, so what the file actually decides is THE BOX.

      `?vol=N` is voxels per L0 cell and **defaults to 0, which builds
      nothing.** The slice view samples the tree directly and owes this pass
      nothing, so until M6.3 has a reader, filling a volume every frame would
      be cost with no consumer. `?volbox=domain|refined` picks the extent and
      `?volBudget=` (MiB, default 256) REFUSES rather than silently
      shrinking.

      **THE BALLPARK WAS MADE BEFORE THE CODE AND THE CODE AGREES WITH IT.**
      On the flagship case (sphere n=16, `?levels=3`), measured against the
      page:

          ?vol=1 &volbox=domain    192x128x128   24.0 MiB   runs
          ?vol=4 &volbox=refined   96^3 over a 24^3 L0 extent
                                   at origin [36,52,52]     6.75 MiB   runs
          ?vol=4 &volbox=domain    768x512x512   1536 MiB   REFUSED

      The refined box is the 24^3 L0 cells the ballpark predicted from
      `debugPoolState`, found independently here by walking `blockSlot`. And
      the refusal FIRES, verified against the page rather than asserted:
      `error: ?vol=4 over domain needs a 768x512x512 volume = 1536 MiB, past
      ?volBudget=256`, with no `window.__D3` -- the same shape M5.0's
      refusal was verified in. So is the mode typo:
      `error: ?volbox=nonsense is not one of domain|refined`.

      GATE MET: `body3-vol` and `body3-vol-refined` in
      `validate-d3-invariants.js`. `debugReadVolume` maps a z-slab back,
      decodes binary16 exactly (subnormals included -- rounding the decoder
      would make the tolerance a property of the decoder), and the tool
      scores every 7th texel against `debugSampleTree` AT THE SAME PHYSICAL
      POINTS. Worst relative error **5.98e-4** over the domain box and
      **8.74e-4** over the refined box, against a bound of 4/1024: both are
      half-precision rounding and nothing else, which is the right answer
      because NEITHER PATH INTERPOLATES -- a disagreement here would be a
      wrong box transform, not a resampling error.

      Two things worth carrying:
      - **`?vol=1` over the domain is a COPY, not a resample**, because
        `VOL_H = 1` with origin 0 puts every voxel centre exactly on an L0
        cell centre. That is what makes the alignment case checkable at all;
        a box-arithmetic error shows up as a shift rather than as blur.
      - **`copyTextureToBuffer` needs 256-byte row alignment**, and an
        unpadded `bytesPerRow` is a validation error -- which on this API is
        silent. `debugReadVolume` pads and strides accordingly.

      **`?vol=` WAS RE-SPECIFIED BY M6.4a and the example above reads
      differently now.** It was "voxels per L0 cell", which is one number
      meaning three things in a three-level run; it is now a MULTIPLE OF EACH
      LEVEL'S OWN RESOLUTION, so `?vol=1` gives every level a volume matching
      its own grid exactly. `?vol=4&volbox=refined` above is today's
      `?vol=1&volbox=refined` and produces the same 96^3 over the same 24^3
      L0 extent. The old phrasing survived until a gate asked for 4 at level
      2 and got sixteen.

      The REFUSAL messages moved with it: the budget is now over the whole
      stack and names every volume in it, since "?vol=2 needs 1536 MiB" with
      no breakdown does not tell you which level to stop asking for.
- [x] **M6.2 — the scalar field. DONE 2026-09-11.**
      `shaders/d3_volume_scalar.wgsl`: Q by centred differences on the
      resampled velocity volume, one pass, one texture in and one out.

      **NOT PER-Q AND WITH NO INCLUDES**, because this kernel never touches a
      population, a tile or the lattice. Naming a velocity set here would be
      the same latent lie `common_d3_render_slice.wgsl`'s header names in the
      opposite case, where the lattice IS reachable and so is declared.

      **rgba16float, NOT the `r32float`-if-`float32-filterable` this entry
      asked for, and the reason is the normalization rather than the
      capability.** Three facts, two of them already measured in the ballpark
      above: `r16float` is not storage-writable in core; `r32float` is, but
      FILTERING it needs an optional feature and the raymarcher wants
      trilinear; `rgba16float` is storage-writable AND filterable in core. The
      objection to half precision is real -- Q runs at ~(U/D)^2 ~ 1e-5, which
      is BELOW binary16's smallest normal (6.1e-5) and would land in the
      subnormals -- and is answered by dividing by the same reference the
      criterion and the slice view already divide by (`d3-criterion.mjs`'s
      qRef, `main-3d.js`'s `U_SCALE`/`V_SCALE`), which puts every channel at
      order 1. "Hand the shader a ratio, not a quantity" is this project's
      existing answer to this exact question, and taking it removes an
      optional-feature dependency the mobile target might not have.

      **HAVING PAID 8 B/VOXEL, THE OTHER THREE CHANNELS ARE FREE, SO THEY ARE
      FILLED**: `(Q/qRef, |omega|/vRef, |u|/uRef, rho - 1)`. One pass produces
      every field the view can show and switching between them is a uniform
      write, not a recompute. `|u|` is worth keeping reachable for the reason
      M6.0b records -- it is the field that CANNOT express a differencing
      artifact, which is what separated a rendering bug from a solver one.

      **THE STENCIL SHORTENS AT A BOX FACE AND REPORTS ITS OWN WIDTH.** A
      sub-box volume has no neighbour outside itself, so the index clamps --
      and a stencil that then divided by the step it ASKED for would be wrong
      by 2x over the whole boundary layer of the volume, which for a refined
      box is exactly the seam region the view exists to look at. That is
      M6.0b's divisor lesson in its cheapest form. The level-0 domain volume
      wraps instead, because there the neighbour is real.

      GATE: `tools/validate-d3-raymarch.js --cases=scalar`. Q is recomputed on
      the HOST from the SAME fp16 velocity texels the kernel differenced,
      through `d3-criterion.mjs`'s `qOfGrad` -- the module
      `tools/test-d3-criterion.js` already scores against closed forms,
      including the one that matters (pure shear is exactly zero). So this is
      the GPU against an independently-tested routine on identical inputs,
      not against a second transcription. Worst disagreement, as a fraction of
      the slab's own max|Q|, on the flagship shape at all three levels:
      **7.2e-4 / 6.4e-4 / 4.5e-4**, against a bound of 1e-2. That is f32
      against f64 in a sum of nine squares plus the binary16 rounding of the
      stored ratio, and nothing else.
- [x] **M6.3 — the raymarcher. DONE 2026-09-11.** `shaders/d3_raymarch.wgsl`,
      a fragment shader over a fullscreen quad: orbit camera, front-to-back
      compositing, trilinear through the scalar texture, `?view=volume`.

      **THE TRANSFER FUNCTION IS NORMALIZED BECAUSE THE VOLUME IS.** M1's
      lesson applies here first -- a transfer function not normalized to the
      actual field renders a correct simulation as a black screen -- and the
      shader is in no position to normalize anything, since it has no idea
      what scale the flow is at. So the SCALAR VOLUME carries the
      normalization (M6.2) and `iso` is a dimensionless number whose default
      is the MEASURED one: `d3-criterion.mjs`'s `Q_THRESHOLD`, 0.1 of the
      body's own shear scale.

      **THE TONE CURVE IS REINHARD, AND `common_vortcolor.wgsl` HAD ALREADY
      ARGUED FOR IT -- the same argument, biting harder.** A hard clip
      collapses everything above the knee to one colour AND to one OPACITY,
      so the front face of the strongest structure goes opaque and the render
      degenerates into a smooth isosurface of the iso value. Measured on a
      Re = 300 sphere, where near-wall Q is over a hundred times the wake's:
      clipped, the boundary layer was a featureless shell and the wake was not
      visible at all; compressed, both are in one picture. That is the entire
      reason to raymarch rather than draw one isosurface, and the first
      version threw it away.

      **THE BODY IS SPHERE-TRACED FROM ITS OWN SDF**, not thresholded out of
      the volume. `d3-body.mjs`'s shapes are TRUE signed distances (M2), which
      is what makes tracing them valid rather than merely plausible, and it
      draws the surface at full precision instead of at whatever resolution
      the volume happens to be.

      **THE CAMERA'S TARGET IS COMPUTED IN THE SHADER FROM THE BODY BUFFER**,
      for M8.3's reason: a host-written target is as stale as the last
      readback, and the picture then slides forward and snaps back every time
      one lands. Same binding, same argument as the slice view's offset.

      GATE, AND IT IS THE ONLY CHECKABLE CLAIM A CAMERA HAS. "Is it pointing
      at the interesting part" has no reference value. But with `?volIso=`
      above every value in the volume the render is the body's SILHOUETTE and
      nothing else, and the host knows exactly where a sphere projects to --
      by a ray/sphere test rather than a projected radius, since a perspective
      camera makes the outline a conic and a radius check would be wrong by
      the same amount everywhere, which is precisely the error that gets
      absorbed into a tolerance. Measured: **IoU 1.000, 120 of 120 pixels,
      zero disagreement**. `d3-volume.mjs` is the host statement of the orbit
      and the pinhole; `tools/test-d3-volume.js` mutation-checks it.

      The transfer function's own gate is an A/B on one page: the field's max
      (15.95 of qRef) against the iso (0.1), and the lit-pixel count with the
      volume (298) against the count with the iso raised above everything
      (120, the body alone). A picture that does not change when the volume is
      switched off is what a render reading an empty texture looks like, and
      "lit pixels > 0" alone cannot tell that from a working one.

#### M6.4 — THE MULTI-LEVEL VOLUME STACK, i.e. "the octree raymarcher"

Scoped 2026-09-10, and **the measurement above turns it from a research
project into an afternoon.** sec 1.3 says "do not attempt to raymarch the
quadtree/octree pool directly -- it is accurate and it is a research
project". That stays true of the POOL. It is not true of the thing actually
wanted, and the two had been conflated.

**THE PROBLEM M6.1-M6.3 CANNOT SOLVE.** A uniform volume must pick ONE
resolution and the hierarchy has several. At L0 the near-body boundary layer
is gone; at the finest level the full domain is 2.25 GB. No single volume
shows the global wake AND the near-wall sheet at once, which is the picture a
refined run exists to produce.

**THE CHEAP ANSWER: ONE DENSE VOLUME PER LEVEL, EACH COVERING ONLY THAT
LEVEL'S BOUNDING BOX.** Refined regions are small by construction -- that is
what AMR means -- and `debugPoolState` already reports the box. For the
flagship case:

    L0   full domain    192x128x128 @1x      36 MB
    L1   32^3 L0 cells   64^3      @2x        3 MB
    L2   24^3 L0 cells   96^3      @4x       10 MB
                                             ------
                                              49 MB, every level at FULL
                                              resolution

against 2.25 GB for the uniform-finest volume it replaces. The raymarcher
then tests a ray sample against 2-3 boxes, innermost first, and takes the
first hit -- **no per-step tree descent, no pointer chasing, no stack.**
That is the entire difference from "raymarch the pool", and it is the
difference between a research project and three more bind groups.

**WHY THE BOXES ARE NOT WASTEFUL, and the number that says so.** A level's
refined set is not its bounding box -- a body-fitted shell is a shell, and
the box is solid. Measured on the flagship: L2 holds 1280 tiles against a
12^3 = 1728-block box, so the box costs **1.35x** the set. The holes are not
wrong, either: the resample pass writes `sampleTree`, which FALLS BACK, so a
hole holds the correct coarser value. Sampling the innermost covering box is
right everywhere, not just where that level exists.

**WHEN THIS BREAKS, and it is worth writing down before it does** (the
successor is M8.5, which is smaller than this paragraph implies). Bounding
boxes work because the refined set is ONE compact region. Geometry-forced
refinement guarantees that -- the body is one body. A Q-criterion or
vorticity criterion does NOT: refinement follows the wake, then breaks into
scattered patches, and a union of bounding boxes degenerates toward the full
domain. That is the configuration where true per-ray octree descent is the
only answer, and it is also the configuration this solver does not yet
produce (sec 1.3's criterion work is unstarted). **So the ordering is: boxes
now, traversal if and when the criterion changes** -- and the trigger is
measurable rather than a judgement call, since the box-union volume against
the refined-set volume is a ratio `debugPoolState` can already report. Above
~4x, boxes have stopped paying.

Staged, gated, and each stage is a small change to M6.1-M6.3 rather than a
parallel path:

- [x] **M6.4a — the stack exists. DONE 2026-09-11.** One `rgba16float`
      volume per level over that level's own bounding box, filled by the SAME
      resample kernel with a different origin, extent and scale, plus a scalar
      volume each. `?volstack=0` keeps the single-volume build as the control.

      **THE BOXES CAME OUT AS THE BALLPARK PREDICTED THEM, to the cell.**
      Flagship case, sphere n=16 at `?levels=3`, found by walking `blockSlot`
      rather than by assuming: L0 192x128x128, L1 32^3 L0 cells at 64^3, L2
      24^3 L0 cells at 96^3.

      **`?vol=` IS A MULTIPLE OF EACH LEVEL'S OWN RESOLUTION, not voxels per
      L0 cell**, and the difference is not cosmetic: level m's grid already
      has 2^m cells per L0 cell, so the old phrasing makes one number mean
      three things in a three-level run. It survived until the `volbox=refined`
      gate asked for 4 at level 2 and got sixteen -- a 256^3 slab where a 96^3
      was meant, which surfaced as a probe-limit error rather than as a wrong
      picture. `?vol=1` now means every level's volume matches its grid
      EXACTLY, so nothing is interpolated and nothing is discarded.

      **THE ORIGIN IS A UNIFORM AND THE SHAPE IS AN OVERRIDE**, which is the
      whole of the dynamic story. A refined box FOLLOWS the body, and
      M4.2b-iii already established that a translating body holds `inUse`
      constant while every tile changes hands -- the SIZE is steady and the
      POSITION is not. Baking the origin in would mean rebuilding the
      pipeline, the texture and the bind group every few frames; instead the
      extent is fixed where it has to be (a texture has a size) and the origin
      slides. A box that OUTGROWS its extent is said out loud in `#status`
      rather than clipped in silence, and `?volMargin=` (2 blocks by default
      on a dynamic run) is the slack it moves within.

      **THE BOX IS THE SMALLEST PERIODIC SPAN, NOT min..max.** With a moving
      window the body travels through a periodic buffer and its shell travels
      with it, so twice a lap the shell STRADDLES THE SEAM -- and taken as
      min..max its bounding box is then the whole axis, which at level 2 of
      the flagship is a 1.5 GB texture request instead of a 10 MB one. So the
      host removes the largest circular GAP, and the raymarcher's box test
      wraps to match; the wrap is a no-op for any box that does not straddle,
      since a wrapped coordinate can only re-enter a box spanning nearly the
      whole axis.

      GATE: `body3-volstack` in `tools/validate-d3-invariants.js`, which is
      the M6.1 check run ONCE PER LEVEL -- each volume scored against
      `debugSampleTree` at its own points, so the per-level origin, extent and
      scale all have to be right at once. Worst relative disagreement
      **6.13e-4 / 6.18e-4 / 6.45e-4** at L0 / L1 / L2, against 4/1024: half
      precision and nothing else. The two single-volume rows either side of it
      (`body3-vol` over the domain, `body3-vol-refined` over the finest box)
      are its ends and still pass unchanged.
- [x] **M6.4b — innermost-box-wins in the raymarcher. DONE 2026-09-11.** The
      stack is ordered coarsest-first and the LAST box containing a sample is
      the answer: two or three box tests, no descent, no pointer chasing, no
      stack. **The step is that box's own voxel size**, which is the payoff
      rather than an optimization bolted on -- a ray takes coarse steps
      through the coarse field and fine steps only through the refined shell,
      so resolving the near-wall sheet costs steps only where that sheet is.

      GATE, and it is the image difference this entry asked for:
      `tools/validate-d3-raymarch.js --cases=stack` renders the same state
      twice, `?volstack=1` against `?volstack=0`, offscreen at a fixed size in
      a fixed format -- deliberately not through the swap chain, which would
      compare two pictures at whatever size and device-pixel-ratio each run
      happened to get. `d3-volume.mjs` then says INDEPENDENTLY which pixels
      are ALLOWED to differ: the ones whose ray touches a refined box, by a
      host ray/AABB test. Measured: **234 pixels differ of 36864, all 234
      inside the allowed set, ZERO outside it**, of 2188 that could. Both
      directions are gated -- nothing differing would mean the stack is not
      being sampled, and something differing outside every box would mean the
      box transform is wrong rather than that the picture got sharper.

      The predicate is only valid where no box straddles the seam, so the tool
      asserts that (`stackUnwrapped`) rather than assuming it: a gate that
      quietly widened its own allowed set until it passed would be no gate.

      AND IT IS VISIBLY SHARPER, which is what it is for: on a close-up of the
      Re = 300 sphere the stacked render shows a thin crisp boundary-layer
      sheet and striations in the near wake where the single volume shows a
      soft blob. Whole-image total variation **+8.5%** (0.5546 against
      0.5111) -- a small number because most of the frame is background and
      body, and quoted as such rather than dressed up.
- [x] **M6.4c — the box-efficiency readout. DONE 2026-09-11.**
      `debugPoolState` reports `boxBlocks` and `boxRatio` per level and
      `tools/validate-d3-invariants.js` prints them on every checkpoint line
      (`box/set L1 1.04x L2 1.14x`). Reported, never gated: the number is the
      trigger, and a threshold on it would turn a design signal into a red
      cell for a configuration that is working as designed.

      Measured on a geometry-forced shell (sphere n=8, `?levels=3`): **1.04x
      at L1 and 1.14x at L2**, against the ~4x at which boxes stop paying and
      against the 1.35x recorded for the flagship. That is the "one compact
      region" assumption holding. M8.4 already measured the other side --
      `boxRatio` **4.6** on a wake-following criterion, above the trigger --
      so the successor case is not hypothetical, it is measured and waiting
      (M8.5).

      **TWO CORRECTIONS, 2026-09-12.** `poolStateAt`'s bbox was a plain
      min..max in BUFFER coordinates, so under a moving window it read the
      seam crossing as geometry: 12.29 at a straddling step on the card
      against ~2.0 away from one. It now uses `axisSpan` and carries `ext`
      explicitly, because a wrapped span has `hi` BELOW `lo` and hi - lo + 1
      goes negative rather than merely wrong. The M8.4 number above SURVIVES
      -- it was measured on a pinned sphere with no window, where min..max and
      the periodic span agree.
      **`debugCriterion` HAD THE SAME MIN..MAX and now uses `axisSpan` too**
      (2026-09-13) -- the per-threshold `bbox`/`boxRatio`, which is the path
      M8.4 measured through. That was the THIRD instance in one file, and it
      would have been wrong for the first wake-criterion number taken on the
      card. M8.4's own 4.6 stands: pinned sphere, no window, where min..max and
      the periodic span agree.

- [x] **M6.4d — the gradient stencil reaches one SOURCE cell. DONE
      2026-09-12.** `?volh=0` restores the old behaviour for the A/B.

      **THE ARTIFACT.** A live `?levels=3` card render at `?view=volume` shows
      crunchy dark stipple along the vortex tubes. `?volstack=0` removes it
      entirely and no other knob does, which places it in the refined
      volumes rather than in the raymarcher or the solver.

      **WHY, AND IT IS TWO THINGS AND NOT ONE.** A level's volume is a dense
      grid over a BOUNDING BOX while the refined set inside it is a shell, so
      most of the box is filled from a COARSER level -- measured 58.1% of the
      structured voxels at L2 on the card at `?levels=3`, with `boxRatio`
      1.88, i.e. the dominant population and not an edge effect. That is the
      first thing, and 96547af found it. It is only a PLATEAU, though: the
      resample samples the tree NEAREST, so every voxel inside one source cell
      holds a bit-identical value, and a plateau by itself renders as a
      plateau. The second thing is what turns it into speckle:
      `d3_volume_scalar.wgsl` differenced every voxel over ONE VOXEL, `VOL_H`
      being a pipeline constant for the whole texture. Across a replicated
      block the central difference is exactly zero, and across the one voxel
      at its edge it is the entire source-cell jump divided by a step
      2^(level-src) too small. Q is a squared velocity gradient, so it squares
      that.

      **THE FIX IS A RULE THIS CODEBASE ALREADY HAD.**
      `common_d3_tree_sample.wgsl`: "any finite difference taken from it must
      use the sampler's own `h`". The gradient pass structurally could not
      obey it, because the resample computed `TreeSample.level` and threw it
      away. It now writes it to a companion volume and the stencil spans one
      source cell:

          stride_k = max(1, round(2^-srcLevel / h_k))   voxels

      stated once in `d3-volume.mjs`'s `stencilStride`, mirrored in
      `strideFor`, and unit-tested on the anisotropic and `?vol=N` cases where
      reading the stride off the LEVEL difference alone is wrong by exactly
      `mult`.

      **THE GATE IS AN EXACT NO-OP CLAIM, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH HAVING.**
      A voxel the refined level actually covers has stride 1 either way, so
      the A/B must be BIT-IDENTICAL there -- not close. Measured by
      `tools/validate-d3-raymarch.js --cases=volh` (sphere n=8, `?levels=3`,
      `?volMargin=2`): **0 of 4096 at-level voxels differ, 5111 of 5120
      replicated ones do**. So M6.4b's sharp near-wall sheet is untouched and
      only the population that was wrong moves. `?volMargin=2` is part of the
      case rather than a tweak: a STATIC run defaults the margin to 0 and a
      small sphere's shell nearly fills its own bbox, which gave a first run
      with 4096 at-level voxels and ZERO replicated ones -- a gate with
      nothing to act on.

      **AND THE MEASUREMENT, on the configuration the artifact appears in**
      (`tools/probe-d3-volume-crunch.js --legs=full,fixedh`, card n=32
      `?levels=3`, 1000 steps). Normalized |lap Q| on the two populations,
      replicated over at-level:

          leg      boxRatio  coarse%   crunch@lvl  crunch<lvl  ratio
          full        1.884      58.1%       0.1318      0.0959   0.73
          fixedh      1.884      59.6%       0.1328      0.4695   3.54

      **4.9x on the population that was wrong, and NOTHING on the one that was
      right.** The replicated voxels fall 0.4695 -> 0.0959 while the at-level
      voxels sit at 0.1328 -> 0.1318 (0.8%, which is the two legs classifying
      slightly different voxels as "structured", not the stencil). The RATIO
      inverting -- 3.54 to 0.73 -- is the sharper statement: the replicated
      population is now SMOOTHER than the refined one, which is what coarse
      data differenced at its own spacing should be, where before it was three
      and a half times rougher. `boxRatio` 1.884 and `inUse` 6192 are IDENTICAL
      across the pair, so the two legs are looking at the same tree.

      **`fixedh` IS THE CONTROL 96547af DID NOT HAVE.** It holds the boxes,
      the set and the mismatch fixed and moves only the stencil, which is what
      separates "the stride is the mechanism" from "something changed". `flat`
      (`?volstack=0`) cannot do that job: it removes the refined DATA along
      with the boxes. `?volh=0` also drops the companion allocation (the
      dummies go everywhere and `?volBudget=` stops counting it), so it is the
      whole pre-M6.4d build and not just its shader -- which is right for a
      control and means the A/B cannot be used to price the memory. Read the
      allocation off the budget line instead.

      **COST: 4 B/voxel, ON THE SMALL BOXES ONLY.** The L0 volume gets a
      1x1x1 dummy and `WRITE_LEVEL`/`VOL_HSRC` fold to 0, because its own
      spacing is already the coarsest in the tree and its stride is 1 by
      construction -- so the flagship pays on the 12 MB volume, not the 50 MB
      one. `rgba8uint` and not `r8uint`: r8 is not storage-writable in core
      WebGPU, the same wall this pass's own header hit with r16float. Counted
      in `?volBudget=`, because an allocation the budget cannot see is one
      that OOMs instead of refusing.

      **AND IT COUPLES TO M6.2's GATE, which now says so.** `hostQSlab`
      differences at a fixed one-voxel stride -- exactly what the kernel does
      for an at-level voxel and NOT what it does for a replicated one -- so
      the `scalar` case now restricts its comparison to the at-level
      population and PRINTS the skipped count. On this config the count is 0
      (a static run defaults `?volMargin=0`, so the box is the shell's tight
      bbox) and every number is unchanged: worst Q 7.20e-4 / 6.35e-4 /
      4.45e-4 at L0 / L1 / L2. It refuses outright if a slab is ever ENTIRELY
      replicated, which is the way that filter could otherwise turn into a
      gate that passes by comparing nothing.

      Whole suite green after the change, at its recorded values: `stack`
      234 allowed / 0 forbidden of 2188, `camera` IoU 1.000, `window` 1.00x.

      **THE OTHER HALF IS M6.4f**, below: correct differencing does not stop
      the box OVERRIDING L0 with a coarser level's data across the majority of
      its extent.

- [x] **M6.4f — a refined box is used only where its voxel came from its own
      level. DONE 2026-09-13.** `?volfallback=0` restores
      innermost-box-wins-unconditionally for the A/B.

      `levelAt` took the innermost containing box whatever was in it, so across
      the part of a box its refined set does not cover it handed the ray a
      coarser level's value replicated onto a fine grid -- and then sampled it
      at the FINE voxel size, paying fine steps and fine interpolation for data
      with neither. It now DESCENDS and takes the first box that both contains
      the point and owns its data, falling through to L0. Descending is what
      bounds the extra companion fetch to the boxes actually entered --
      typically one, none outside them.

      **9.36% of pixels change, worst 37/255.** A same-build repeat differs in
      ZERO pixels, so that is the change and not run-to-run drift.

      **THE COST IS BELOW THE FLOOR, and getting to that took three wrong
      measurements.** See the perf note below; |cost| < ~6% of a 1024^2 render.

      **PERF NOTE, and it is the transferable part: NEVER PRICE A SHADER ON THE
      ANIMATION THREAD, AND OFF-THREAD IS NOT SUFFICIENT EITHER.**
      - A frame rate off the rAF loop is the DISPLAY's. 1152x720 read 59.9 fps
        on both legs; 2560x1440 read 59.9 fps on both legs. "Equal" was
        "capped", twice, and raising the resolution did not escape it because
        the frame costs under 16.6 ms either way.
      - `--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit` separates the legs
        (115.7 against 119.2) but puts the browser's frame pacing in the
        number, and that reading said the fallback was 2.9% SLOWER where the
        off-thread one says 3.9% faster. Neither survives the noise floor; the
        sign is not even stable.
      - `debugRenderFrame` is off the animation thread and STILL cannot time
        this: **1181 ms per call at 1024^2 against a 4.7 ms draw**. It is not
        the copy, it is base64 of 4 MiB and the CDP round trip. That tool makes
        an IMAGE.
      - `debugBenchRender` is the instrument: N draws in ONE command buffer,
        one submit, `onSubmittedWorkDone`, no presentation and no readback,
        minimum of its batches. **Its own floor is 6.2%** on a same-build
        repeat with a fresh Chrome per leg, which is what bounds the answer.
        Tightening it means A/B-ing both pipelines inside ONE page.

- [x] **M6.4e — a refined box's placement moves ATOMICALLY. DONE
      2026-09-13.** `?volsync=0` restores the old stagger for the A/B.

      **THE REPORT.** On the live page, the fluid near the plate is right but
      further out it shifts EN MASSE by a discrete offset for a moment and
      snaps back, occasionally. Present with M6.4d on and off, so not the
      stencil.

      **TWO BUFFERS, ONE POSITION.** A refined box's placement is stated
      twice: the resample's `originBuf`, which decides what is written INTO
      the texture, and the ray params' `boxLo`, which decides where the
      raymarcher reads it OUT of world space. They have to be published
      together. `refreshVolumeBoxesNow` read and applied one level at a time
      and called `writeRayParams()` once at the END, so the sequence was

          write originBuf(L1)  ->  await readBlockSlot(L2)  ->  writeRayParams()

      and every frame the rAF loop drew inside that await rendered L1's
      freshly resampled contents through L1's stale placement -- displaced by
      exactly the distance the box had just moved, which is a whole number of
      L0 cells.

      **A DEPTH BUG, the same family as `serializedOn`'s.** At `?levels=2` the
      loop body runs once and its only await is BEFORE its only write, so
      there is no gap to observe. It takes a second refined level to exist.

      **AND THE APPLY ORDER IS WHY IT READS AS A FAR-FIELD FAULT.** The
      innermost box is applied LAST, with nothing but synchronous code between
      its write and the publish, so the level covering the body is always in
      register. The one that is not is L1 -- 80x72x112 L0 cells of a
      192x160x192 domain at `?volMargin=4`, against L2's 56x48x88. The
      symptom's geometry is the loop's order, not the flow's.

      **MEASURED, and the counter is the gate** (`tools/probe-d3-volbox-sync.js`,
      card `?levels=3`, 150 s per leg). `debugVolBoxSync().partialFrames` is
      the number of rendered frames drawn between a box's origin write and the
      ray params that place it:

          leg          updates  partialFrames  maxGapMs  frames
          volsync=0        125     710 (16.3%)     362.2    4357
          volsync=1        132       0              0.2     4355

      Same update count, same frame count: the fix is free. Zero BY
      CONSTRUCTION with the reads hoisted, which is exactly why it is counted
      rather than asserted -- "there is no await here" is one refactor away
      from being false.

      **IT ALSO REMOVES A SECOND INCONSISTENCY** the per-level version had:
      innermost-box-wins means a correctly-placed L2 could be composited over
      a stale L1, so the two levels disagreed with each other and not merely
      with the truth.

      **NOTHING IN THE STANDING SUITE COVERS IT**, and that is structural, not
      an omission to fix later: the race is between an UNAWAITED refresh and
      the frame loop, and every tool here drives the page through
      `debugStepSync`, which has no frame loop. It takes a live page and real
      elapsed wall time -- the shape plans/3D.md already records for
      `serializedOn`'s map race. The probe is the instrument; the page keeps
      the count so a live run can be asked at any time.

#### M6.5 — FILMING IT

Added 2026-09-12. Not a gate; a PRODUCER, in the sense `tools/probe-d3-*.js`
and `tools/bench-*.js` are. A viewer that can only be looked at live is a
viewer nobody else can look at.

- [x] **M6.5 — the movie mode. DONE 2026-09-12.**
      `tools/render-d3-movie.js` drives `index-3d.html` forward a DECLARED
      number of steps per frame, renders each frame through
      `debugRenderFrame` (M6.4b's offscreen path, generalized), and pipes raw
      RGBA straight into ffmpeg. No temp PNGs, no screen recorder, no
      compositor.

          node tools/render-d3-movie.js                      # the card, 30 s
          node tools/render-d3-movie.js --view=slice --seconds=10 --fps=24
          node tools/render-d3-movie.js --scenario=sphere --spin=90 --gif

      **A SCREEN RECORDER WOULD FILM THE SOLVER'S FRAME RATE, NOT THE
      FLOW'S**, and that is the whole argument for doing it this way. The
      page advances `?spf=` steps per animation frame and paints when it can,
      so a recording's time axis is whatever the GPU managed that second: a
      clip of an expensive configuration runs SLOW, and two clips of two
      builds are not comparable. Here `--seconds` and `--fps` describe the
      FILM and `--steps` describes the FLOW, so a second of footage is a
      stated number of solver steps and stays one on another machine. The
      other two properties follow from the same choice -- the frame size is
      what was asked for rather than whatever the window happened to be, and
      `debugStepSync` returns before the render, so the state filmed is the
      state that was asked for rather than whatever the animation loop had
      reached.

      **THE SLICE VIEW FILMS TOO**, through a second pipeline in the
      offscreen format, built on first use so that the dozens of validation
      configs which create the canvas pipeline do not also create this one.
      It is not the more interesting picture of a falling plate, but it is
      the picture this project debugs with, and a movie mode that could film
      only one of the two views would send someone back to a screen recorder
      for the other. `debugRenderFrame` is now the one entry point and
      `debugRenderVolume` is gone; `renderConsts` is one statement of the
      slice view's overrides, because two pipelines from two copies of it is
      238e48c's shape again.

      **THE SLICE'S HEIGHT FOLLOWS ITS PLANE**, which is `resize()`'s rule
      for the canvas and the same distortion either way -- a slice drawn to
      an aspect that is not the plane's renders a sphere as an ellipse. The
      tool believes the PAGE about the size it got rather than the size it
      asked for, because an encoder fed a size the frames are not produces a
      torn picture rather than an error.

      It REPORTS, from a 150-byte body readback every frame: how far the body
      fell, how far it drifted across, and how much it turned. A clip of a
      tumbling plate that does not tumble is worth being TOLD rather than
      left to notice.

      **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT REPORT WAS WRONG IN EXACTLY THE WAY
      THAT MATTERS HERE.** It integrated `|omega|`, which is ARC LENGTH: a
      plate rocking back and forth accumulates it without turning over at
      all, and the default card was duly reported as having "turned 0.44
      revolutions", which is true of the arc and false of the plate. The fix
      is TWO numbers rather than a better one --

          net = |INT omega dt| / 2pi     the VECTOR integral, in which
                                        opposite swings cancel
          arc = INT |omega| dt / 2pi     how far it turned, sign ignored

      -- with tumbling being `net ~ arc` and fluttering `arc >> net`. Neither
      is read from the QUATERNION, which says only where the plate ended up
      and reads exactly zero for one that made exactly one revolution.

      **AND THE ANSWER IT GIVES IS THAT THE CARD DOES NOT TUMBLE**, which
      confirms D4's "neither coupling tumbling" with a number instead of an
      impression. n = 32, 26000 steps, diffuse coupling, five configurations
      chosen to attack it from every side the scenario offers:

          case                            fell  drift   net rev  arc rev  net/arc
          Re 500, tilt 0.15 (the default)  798   20.3     0.04     0.44     0.10
          Re 500, tilt 0.70                785   16.0     0.12     0.76     0.16
          Re 500, tilt 0.70, levels=2      847   22.1     0.10     1.03     0.10
          Re 500, tilt 0.70, span 2        764   15.8     0.09     0.91     0.09
          Re 1100, tilt 0.70, levels=2     836    7.9     0.07     1.80     0.04
          Re 1100, tilt 0.70, span 2       790   21.4     0.18     2.41     0.07

      ALL SIX FLUTTER, and the trend is the informative part: `arc` grows
      monotonically from 0.44 to 1.80 as the initial tilt is raised, the body
      is sharpened (`?levels=2` halves the chi band), the span is doubled
      toward the 2D limit tumbling is established in, and Re is taken to the
      TARGET -- while `net` stays pinned between 0.04 and 0.12 throughout.
      More Reynolds number and a sharper body buy a more violent ROCK and not
      one more degree of turning over.

      That is the shape of answer which says the REGIME is wrong rather than
      the initial condition. It is also the old metric's trap sprung in
      public: `arc = 1.80` would have read as "tumbling" under any threshold
      at or below one and three quarter turns.

      **THE 2:1 PLATE AT THE TARGET Re IS THE CLOSEST ANY OF THEM COMES, AND
      IT IS THE VELOCITY THAT SAYS SO RATHER THAN THE ROTATION.** `net` is
      0.18 revolutions -- 4.5x the default's and the largest here -- and `arc`
      at 2.41 is also the largest, so doubling the span does push toward
      tumbling. But 0.18 of a turn is 65 degrees, which is flutter by any
      threshold. What is genuinely new is that it GLIDES: v = (0.024, -0.022,
      0.009), i.e. a lateral speed NEARLY EQUAL to the descent, against the
      square plate's (0.031, 0.003, 0.003), which falls essentially straight
      down. A plate that has picked a direction and translates along it is the
      fluttering-with-GLIDE regime, which in Pesavento & Wang sits immediately
      next to tumbling.

      That also says the axis being pushed on may be the wrong one. `i_star`
      is 0.17 throughout because `card-params.mjs` fixes it, and the
      tumbling/fluttering boundary in that phase diagram is a CURVE IN
      I*-Re -- not a Reynolds threshold at fixed I*. Span and Re have now both
      been swept with the same answer.

      **AND THE INITIAL CONDITION IS NOT THE OBSTACLE -- FLUTTER IS AN
      ATTRACTOR HERE.** The 2D plate oscillates about horizontal for a long
      time before one SWOOP initiates tumbling, so the natural thing to try
      before sweeping anything is releasing the plate near VERTICAL and
      skipping the wind-up. `?tilt=1.047` (60 degrees from flat, i.e. 30 from
      vertical) on the 2:1 plate at Re = 1100, with the rotation integrated
      every 100 steps instead of only at the end:

          step     fell   net rev  arc rev  net/arc   |omega|
            2000     51     0.221    0.221     1.00   1.33e-3
            4000    118     0.223    0.448     0.50   1.23e-3
            6000    171     0.048    0.678     0.07   6.90e-4
            8000    226     0.306    0.940     0.33   1.05e-4
           12000    349     0.238    1.412     0.17   8.50e-4
           16000    477     0.095    1.755     0.05   7.88e-4
           20000    597     0.056    2.180     0.03   2.39e-4
           26000    789     0.120    2.736     0.04   8.55e-4

      **THE SWOOP HAPPENS, IMMEDIATELY, AND IT IS NOT ENOUGH.** The first 2000
      steps are net/arc = **1.00** -- 79 degrees of rotation ALL ONE WAY,
      which is more net rotation than any other configuration reaches in its
      whole 26000 steps. The release IS the swoop, and the onset time is duly
      cut from "never" to 2000 steps. Then `net` falls back and oscillates in
      a BOUNDED BAND of 0.05 to 0.31 for the remaining 24000 steps while `arc`
      climbs linearly to 2.74. It does not wind up; it relaxes onto the same
      limit cycle a shallow release reaches.

      So the same attractor is found from `tilt` = 0.15 and from 1.047 -- an
      eightfold change in the release angle, including one that starts the
      plate most of the way to edge-on. That is what makes it a statement
      about the REGIME rather than about where the plate was let go, and it is
      the thing worth knowing before spending a sweep: **`i_star` is the axis
      left**, and the boundary in Pesavento & Wang is a curve in I*-Re.

      **ONE THING ABOUT THE METRIC, learned here.** `net` is the MAGNITUDE of
      a vector integral, so under an oscillation it wanders back toward zero
      and its FINAL value is a sample of a wandering quantity -- 0.120 here
      against a maximum of 0.306. Flutter and tumbling are told apart by
      whether `net` GROWS WITHOUT BOUND, which the running series shows and a
      single end-of-run number does not. Any future sweep should read the
      series.

      **AND THE I* SWEEP DOES NOT CROSS THE BOUNDARY EITHER.**
      `tools/probe-d3-tumble.js --sweep=i_star=0.34,0.68`, on the 2:1 plate at
      Re = 1100 released 30 degrees off vertical -- i.e. on top of every other
      thing that helped:

          i*     rho_b   fell   net end   NET MAX    arc   growth/1k
          0.17     2.7    789     0.120     0.306    2.74      ~0
          0.34     5.4    784     0.326     0.353    2.99   -0.0038
          0.68    10.7    847     0.308     0.361    2.69   +0.0127

      **A FOURFOLD CHANGE IN INERTIA MOVES THE PEAK NET ROTATION BY 18%**, and
      the band is otherwise the same one every configuration has sat in. The
      +0.0127 growth at 4x is 0.33 revolutions over the run, which is inside
      the oscillation amplitude -- the fit is reading the phase, not a trend.
      The parameter is certainly live: `rho_b` doubles with each step (2.7,
      5.4, 10.7) and `gEff` halves to hold the terminal velocity.

      **FOUR AXES ARE NOW SWEPT WITH ONE ANSWER** -- span (1 to 2), Re (500 to
      1100), release angle (0.15 to 1.047 rad), inertia (0.17 to 0.68). At
      that point it stops being a parameter search.

      **THE CONCRETE STRUCTURAL SUSPECT IS THE PLATE'S EFFECTIVE THICKNESS,
      and D4 already measured the mechanism.** These runs use the DIFFUSE
      (chi) coupling, which "is a REGULARIZATION, not a wrong model" but makes
      the body the wrong SIZE. The card is **4 CELLS THICK** at n = 32 with
      aspect 0.125, and the chi band is 0.75 coarse cells per side at
      `?levels=2` -- so the plate is effectively ~5.5 cells, i.e. **aspect
      0.17 against a nominal 0.125, a 37% thickening**. Aspect ratio is a
      primary parameter of the flutter/tumble boundary and a fatter plate
      flutters, so the body being swept may not be the body intended.

      Tested two ways, and NEITHER CROSSES IT:

      **`?bounceback=1` IS NOT AVAILABLE AT THIS tau.** The sharp-body leg blew
      up around step 5000 -- `|v|` pinned at the body's own v_max clamp of 0.2
      and the page stopped advancing -- which is D4's "the moving-body coupling
      degrades as tau -> 1/2" at tau = 0.50436, the worst end of it. So the
      exact-thickness test is unavailable at the target Reynolds number without
      something else changing, and that is a fact about the SOLVER rather than
      about the plate.

      **`?levels=3` HALVES THE BAND AND CHANGES ONLY THE ARC.** Same chi
      coupling, band 0.375 coarse cells instead of 0.75, everything else
      identical:

          band (coarse cells)   net max   arc    growth/1k
          0.75  (levels=2)        0.306   2.74      ~0
          0.375 (levels=3)        0.341   3.51    +0.0074

      The arc rate is up 28% and the peak net rotation is where it has sat in
      every configuration since the beginning. **Sharpening the body makes the
      rocking more vigorous without unbinding it** -- which is the same
      signature refining gave at Re = 500, and is now the second time a change
      that should matter has moved `arc` and not `net`.

      **AND PRE-THINNING THE PLATE TO COMPENSATE DOES NOT CROSS IT EITHER.**
      `?aspect=0.1016` at n = 32 puts 3.25 nominal cells plus 0.75 of band on
      4.0, so for the first time in this search the body being simulated is
      the body `card-params.mjs` specifies. `rho_b` rises to 3.31 as it must
      -- holding I* fixed while thinning the plate makes it denser.

          effective thickness      net max   arc
          ~4.75 (levels=2)           0.306   2.74
          ~4.40 (levels=3)           0.341   3.51
           4.00 (levels=3, thinned)  0.367   3.85

      **EVERY LEVER MOVES `arc` AND LEAVES `net` BOUNDED.** Arc is up 40%
      across the thickness axis; net max is up 20% and its increments are
      DECELERATING (+0.035 then +0.026), converging on roughly 0.4 rather
      than climbing toward a revolution. Extrapolation says no amount of
      further sharpening crosses it.

      **SIX LEVERS, ONE ANSWER**: span (1 -> 2), Re (500 -> 1100), release
      angle (0.15 -> 1.047 rad), inertia (0.17 -> 0.68), chi band
      (0.75 -> 0.375) and the thickness compensation. Each makes the plate
      rock harder and none makes it turn over. That consistency is the
      finding: the plate is being driven harder and something is preventing
      the rotation from running away.

      **THE STRUCTURAL SUSPECT LEFT IS THAT THIS IS A FINITE-SPAN PLATE AND
      TUMBLING IS A 2D PHENOMENON.** Pesavento & Wang is a two-dimensional
      study -- an infinite span, no tips. A finite plate sheds TIP VORTICES,
      which are the classic suppressor of autorotation, and even at span 2 the
      aspect ratio is 2 rather than infinite. The trend across the span sweep
      points the same way: 1 -> 2 raised net max and the glide appeared.

- [x] **M6.5c -- THE STRIP TUMBLES. DONE 2026-09-13, branch `3d/periodic-plate`.**
      Avenue 2 of the list above, built as `?spanfill=1` on the card, plus
      two things it turned out to need. The ladder, each leg one run of
      `tools/probe-d3-tumble.js` (n = 32, Re = 1100, I* = 0.17, tilt 1.047,
      chi, dynamic refinement, 30000 steps):

          body                                 levels  net max  arc   net/arc  verdict
          finite plate, span 2 (M6.5's best)     2      0.31    2.4    0.08    flutter
          STRIP, sharp edges                     2      0.44    2.8    0.08    flutter (0.42 rev one way
                                                                               in 3000 steps, then an
                                                                               edge-on dive at 2x u_t,
                                                                               then rocking)
          STRIP, stadium edges (?edge=1)         2      1.03    3.6    0.28    intermittent -- net
                                                                               drifts up through 1 rev
                                                                               with big excursions
          STRIP, stadium edges                   3      4.14    5.9    0.70    TUMBLING: steady 0.22
                                                                               rev / 1000 steps from
                                                                               step 14000 on, 3.5 turns
